# YOW Product Roadmap

This is the canonical planning source for Your Own World.

All feature planning, launch triage, blocker decisions, and roadmap changes should be made here first. Other planning artifacts must either link back to this file or be treated as historical/generated output.

## Agent Instructions

Codex, Claude, and any other project agent should use this file as the single planning document.

- For every meaningful project change, update this roadmap in the same turn when the change affects scope, status, blockers, bugs, next actions, ownership, or launch readiness.
- When QA is deferred, add the required checks to [docs/QA_PLAN.md](docs/QA_PLAN.md) in the same turn and keep building from this roadmap.
- When the work requires user action, user testing, a product decision, credentials, external setup, or any manual verification that cannot be completed by the agent, provide the user with clear step-by-step instructions at that point in the process.
- Do not create a separate roadmap, backlog, launch list, or planning document unless the user explicitly asks for an export or temporary working draft.
- YOW is not aiming for a minimal MVP. Final paid/public launch means the desired final product scope for the 6 active project types is complete enough to sell without relying on continual post-launch feature development.
- If the product decision is "we want this in YOW," treat it as launch scope unless it is explicitly excluded from the final product, belongs to a future business expansion, or is parked by a direct product decision.
- When a completed task closes or changes a roadmap item, update its status and next action before finishing.
- If no roadmap update is needed, briefly say why in the final response.
- Never run the dev server, or do a `git checkout`/`git reset`/`git stash pop` you expect to persist, with cwd set to the main checkout (`/Users/bishop/Desktop/Claude/yow` itself). Always use a dedicated worktree instead — the main checkout is shared with other concurrent agent sessions, and a branch switch there silently breaks anything relying on its working-tree state (a running dev server, an open browser tab). If a user reports recent work "missing" or "gone" after loading a dev URL, check which branch/commit is actually checked out where that dev server's process has its cwd (`ps aux`, `lsof -i :<port>`) before concluding data was lost — a shared-checkout branch switch is a more likely and fully recoverable cause (see the 2026-08-25 incident this rule came from).

## Phase 0 Rules

### Master Roadmap

Status: Active

Acceptance criteria:

- One canonical roadmap exists with Active, Launch Required, Future/Excluded, Icebox, and Bugs sections.
- Scattered planning notes are moved here or linked from here.
- Generated artifacts, public roadmap database seeds, and acceptance-matrix documents do not override this file.

Linked/historical sources:

- [YOW_MVP_Acceptance_Criteria_Matrix.docx](../YOW_MVP_Acceptance_Criteria_Matrix.docx) is the generated acceptance-matrix document.
- [scripts/create_yow_mvp_matrix.py](../scripts/create_yow_mvp_matrix.py) is the script that generated the initial matrix content.
- [supabase/migrations/20260521_roadmap.sql](../supabase/migrations/20260521_roadmap.sql) seeds the public-facing app roadmap. It is display data, not the product planning source of truth.

### Final Product Scope Discipline

Status: Active

Product stage definition:

- Public beta is the build-and-test period. It may expose incomplete workflows if they are clearly labeled, data-safe, and not misleading.
- Final launch is the fully specced website. The intent is to implement the desired full product now, during beta, and avoid relying on continual post-launch development to finish core feature promises.
- "Launch blocker" still means the issue must be fixed before any public paid/final launch. During beta, the same blocker policy is used to decide whether a beta build is safe enough to keep using.
- Paid/final launch must not market any of the 6 active project types as beta, limited, or coming soon. If an active project type is not fully ready, launch waits or the product scope must be explicitly changed.
- Real paid checkout should remain disabled or treated as test-only until the Launch Readiness Gate below is passed.

Launch scope rule:

- YOW is being built as the final paid launch product, not a narrow MVP.
- Desired product capabilities are launch scope by default.
- Only move a desired capability out of launch scope when there is an explicit product decision that it is a future expansion, excluded business model, or not part of YOW's paid promise.
- Do not hide a desired launch capability in Future/Excluded just because it is complex.

Parking-lot process:

- New ideas still go to Icebox until the user makes a product decision.
- Once the user confirms "we want this," move it into launch scope with acceptance criteria unless they explicitly say it is future/excluded.
- Future/Excluded is for capabilities outside the final product promise, such as collaboration, marketplace/community, native mobile apps, or other future business lines.

Launch blocker definition:

Only data loss, broken auth, broken save, broken export, unusable editor, unusable responsive layout, or missing legal/payment essentials are technical blockers for beta safety. For final paid/public launch, incomplete desired product capabilities also block launch if they are part of the final product promise.

### Launch Readiness Gate

Status: Active

YOW is launch-ready only when every gate below is passed. This is the operating definition for "we can confidently take money."

| Gate | Required Outcome | Status |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Scope gate | All items in Launch Required and To Be Implemented are either complete and QA-passed, or explicitly moved to Future/Excluded by product decision. | **Blocked — 2026-09-01 audit:** the paid desktop promise is not implemented; the signed-in desktop runtime contains Account Settings rather than the project workspace. Other Launch Required rows remain open. |
| Project-type gate | Novel, Novella, Short Story, D&D Campaign, TTRPG Campaign, and Comic/Graphic Novel are all fully live for their promised workflows. No active launch type is marketed as beta/limited at final launch. | **Blocked — 2026-09-01 audit:** automated creation passes, but Comic remains marketed as Beta and the deferred Comic/campaign-specific sign-off is incomplete. |
| Data safety gate | Auth, save, autosave, restore, export, account isolation, local/cloud transition, and storage-limit behavior are QA-passed with no known data-loss blocker. | **Blocked — 2026-09-01 audit:** asynchronous local durability (P0-07, fixed 2026-09-02, needs live-QA — see Security table), project-import ID collisions (P0-06, fixed), non-transactional replace/restore/delete, and reconciliation risks are open. |
| Export ownership gate | Users can export complete project data through ZIP/DOCX/PDF/World Bible where promised, including before cloud expiry or inactive-account cleanup. | **Blocked — 2026-09-01 audit:** export generation passes smoke tests, but restore/import identity is unsafe and visual PDFs silently embed complete project JSON, including non-visible data. |
| Payment gate | Stripe test-mode QA passes for Monthly, Lifetime, Founder, and £6 hosting renewal; live keys/prices/webhooks are reviewed before real checkout is enabled. | **Blocked — 2026-09-01 audit supersedes the 2026-07-28 product acceptance:** editable `user_metadata` can grant paid/desktop entitlement; webhook fulfillment has no idempotency ledger and can extend maintenance on both Checkout and first Invoice; Founder allocation is not atomic; client/FAQ/migration/Stripe price sources drift. Keep real checkout disabled. |
| Legal and promise gate | Pricing, FAQ, Terms, privacy, cancellation, storage caps, Local Mode, cloud expiry, Founder recognition, and export ownership copy match the roadmap decisions. | **Blocked — 2026-09-01 audit:** desktop, AI transport, backup/sync, quota, PDF export, pricing, cookies/storage, and Founder promises do not consistently match behavior. |
| Responsive gate | Core workflows work at mobile, tablet, and desktop widths without hidden critical controls or broken layouts. | **Open / automation red — 2026-09-01 audit:** 58 browser tests pass, 4 skip, and the mobile/tablet writing tests fail on the removed `.ms-textarea` selector; screenshots show the current editor but the gate is not green. |
| Performance gate | A realistic large project remains usable for dashboard, writing, search, worldbuilding, map builder, and exports. | **Blocked — 2026-09-01 audit:** the main bundle is about 2.49 MB minified / 642 KB gzip plus 439 KB CSS, intended dynamic imports are ineffective, and final realistic-load sign-off remains deferred. |
| Marketing gate | Homepage, features, FAQ, pricing, SEO schema, and public pages do not imply excluded features such as collaboration, public sharing, read-only player views, fantasy calendar engines, mobile apps, marketplace/community, publishing integrations, or live VTT play. D&D/TTRPG copy is positioned for prep-focused solo GMs/private campaign planning. | **Blocked — 2026-09-01 audit:** current Home copy says DM notes are hidden from players and players discover lore/map pins progressively despite the explicit no-player-view scope; other plan/AI/data claims drift. |

Launch triage decision (2026-07-04): feature-freeze the Map Builder for new depth until the full QA checklist passes or specific defects require small corrective changes. Schedule polish is accepted into launch scope as a practical configurable story/campaign calendar, but still not marketed as a full invented-calendar engine. Local storage is accepted into launch scope only through the downloadable desktop app/local vault; browser accounts require Cloud Mode. Collaboration remains excluded.

Paid-launch owner decisions (2026-09-02): external solicitor/legal review is recommended but **not required** for launch. The Legal and promise gate requires complete, accurate, owner-approved customer terms and disclosures; it does not require third-party professional sign-off. The macOS and Windows desktop packages will remain **unsigned at paid launch** until an owner-defined revenue threshold can cover OS-level signing/notarization costs. Paid launch therefore requires accurate pre-purchase disclosure plus clean-machine verification of the documented Gatekeeper/SmartScreen install steps, package hashes/provenance, and the already-implemented cryptographically signed Tauri updater — not Apple notarization or Windows publisher signing. OS-level signing becomes a post-threshold operational upgrade rather than a launch blocker.

### Whole-codebase audit snapshot — 2026-09-01

Status: **Paid/public launch blocked. Unrestricted public beta also blocked until the exposed server endpoints, editable entitlement, committed credential, and highest-risk data paths are corrected.**

The detailed evidence, severity, validation output, redundancy review, continuity assessment, and remediation sequence are in [YOW_CODE_AUDIT_2026-09-01.md](YOW_CODE_AUDIT_2026-09-01.md). The consolidated customer-facing copy inventory and conflict checklist are in [marketing-copy-review/](marketing-copy-review/README.md).

Immediate order:

1. Rotate/remove the committed real-looking test credential; add CI secret scanning. **Partial (2026-09-01):** code-side scrub and CI `secret-scan` job (`gitleaks`, plus a custom low-entropy password rule) done on `fix/scrub-committed-test-credential` ([PR #38](https://github.com/bishop7124-ctrl/YoW/pull/38)), covering **two** distinct leaked accounts, not one: `mbishoptesting@gmail.com` (in `scripts/seed-test-data.mjs` and a stray committed `memory/` directory the original audit didn't catch, `334d744`) and `accounta@yourownworld.co.uk` (in a static `docs/design/backups/*.bak` roadmap snapshot). Still open: rotating both live credentials and the git-history rewrite, both pending the user — see [docs/QA_PLAN.md](QA_PLAN.md) Priority -1.
2. Fail closed and authorize/rate-limit the AI proxy, cron mailer, reset/welcome/re-engagement functions, and signed unsubscribe actions. **AI proxy partial (2026-09-01, `fix/ai-proxy-authz`, audit P0-02):** `api/ai-proxy.js` now requires a signed-in session, rejects Free-plan users server-side, enforces a new durable per-user rate limit (`ai_proxy_requests` table), clamps `maxTokens`, and restricts CORS to an explicit origin allowlist (including Vercel preview deployments). Security review caught and this branch fixed a real bypass in its own first draft: the entitlement check initially trusted client-editable `user_metadata` via `getMembership()`'s display-layer fallback — every Free user has no `app_metadata` subscription fields, so any signed-in Free user could have self-granted access with one console call. Fixed with a `hasAiEntitlement()` check that trusts only server-controlled `app_metadata`/`created_at`. Full detail and remaining live-QA items in [docs/QA_PLAN.md](QA_PLAN.md) Priority -1. **Cron mailer/email endpoints partial (2026-09-01, `fix/email-endpoint-authz`, audit P0-03):** `api/send-reengagement-emails.js` now fails closed when `CRON_SECRET` is unset (was silently public); `send-reengagement-email` requires the service-role key; `send-reset-email` allowlists `redirectTo` (closes an open-redirect risk in a real recovery link), adds a durable per-email rate limit, and no longer leaks whether an email has an account; `send-welcome-email` no longer trusts a caller-supplied email at all, deriving it only from a verified service-role or session-token source; the re-engagement unsubscribe link is now HMAC-signed instead of a bare user UUID. Not attempted: a committed `supabase/config.toml` (no such file exists at all — separate finding #26) and true action-link expiry beyond Supabase's own built-in recovery-token TTL. Full detail and remaining live-QA items in [docs/QA_PLAN.md](QA_PLAN.md) Priority -1. **2026-09-02, confirmed by the user: `supabase/migrations/20260901_ai_proxy_rate_limits.sql` and `supabase/migrations/20260901120000_email_action_rate_limits.sql` have been applied to production, and `REENGAGEMENT_UNSUBSCRIBE_SECRET` has been set in both Vercel and Supabase.** The remaining live-QA items above (real rate-limit triggering, real cross-origin block, real unsubscribe-link round trip, etc.) have not been separately re-confirmed — schema/config being live is not the same as those specific behavior checks having been run.
3. Remove all entitlement trust in editable `user_metadata`; allowlist profile updates; migrate existing metadata.
4. Keep paid checkout disabled; add Stripe event idempotency, one fulfillment path per product, atomic Founder allocation, and a tested approved price matrix.
5. Remap every entity/reference during project import and make restore/replace/delete transaction-like before promoting archive safety.
6. Redesign IndexedDB/desktop queued persistence around durable acknowledgement, recovery, and failure injection; stop copying credentials into the desktop vault.
7. Repair/rebaseline Supabase migrations and prove a from-zero environment build.
8. Implement the full advertised desktop workspace or explicitly remove it from launch scope/copy; then sign/notarize and verify platform-complete updates.

The deferred/manual verification required after remediation is recorded at the top of [QA_PLAN.md](QA_PLAN.md). Do not mark an affected gate passed from code review alone.

Launch triage decision (2026-07-04): feature-freeze the Map Builder for new depth until the full QA checklist passes or specific defects require small corrective changes. Schedule polish is accepted into launch scope as a practical configurable story/campaign calendar, but still not marketed as a full invented-calendar engine. Local storage is accepted into launch scope only through the downloadable desktop app/local vault; browser accounts require Cloud Mode. Collaboration remains excluded.

## Competitive Positioning Risks

These are known places where YOW is behind more mature writing/worldbuilding and TTRPG tools. Treat them as launch-positioning constraints unless a direct product decision moves one into launch scope.

| Area | Competitive Gap | Product Decision / Launch Implication | Next Action |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Collaboration and sharing | YOW has no collaboration, shared workspace, public project sharing, or read-only player view. LegendKeeper, Kanka, and World Anvil all offer at least some player/public viewing path, and many GM buyers expect this. | This is a deliberate exclusion from final launch. D&D/TTRPG marketing must target prep-focused solo GMs and private campaign planning, not group campaign portals, shared player bibles, or live table use. | Audit D&D/TTRPG marketing copy before paid launch for solo-GM/private-prep positioning. Treat a future read-only share link/player-view as the single highest-value item on the excluded/future list, but do not promise it at launch. |
| Fantasy calendars | YOW has eras, timelines, history, and Schedule, but no full fantasy calendar simulation engine. Kanka and World Anvil offer invented-calendar systems that some TTRPG users actively use. | Schedule polish is launch scope: configurable month/week lengths and labels. A full invented-calendar engine with leap rules, moons, conversions, and complex recurrence is not launch scope unless explicitly re-decided. | Keep marketing language to "Schedule" or "story/campaign calendar" rather than "fantasy calendar engine." |
| Maturity and trust | Competitors have years of hardening, user communities, reviews, tutorials, and social proof. YOW starts with little public trust. | Founder tier and beta-user seeding are the correct trust instruments; product copy should be honest about ownership, export, and focused scope rather than pretending to have incumbent scale. | Seed Founder/beta users before broader paid launch where possible; collect testimonials, screenshots, and real project examples for marketing after consent. |
| Map builder polish | YOW's map builder is ambitious and drawing-based, but it is also one of the highest-risk/stability subsystems. Dedicated tools and LegendKeeper-style map workflows may feel more polished. | Feature-freeze new depth until QA passes. The launch promise remains planning-grade maps with safe persistence/export, not a full dedicated cartography suite or VTT. | Prioritize authenticated QA, persistence/export, predictable selection/drawing, and responsive safety over adding more map features. |
| Web-only and local-first storage | Browser-based drafting will not satisfy every Scrivener-style user who wants native desktop files and no browser dependency. | Product decision 2026-07-04: the Lifetime tier becomes a downloadable desktop app with a local project vault (see PRD: Desktop Lifetime App and Local Vault), directly answering the Scrivener-style objection. Free/Monthly remain web-only. Do not advertise the desktop app publicly until it is real (Phase 6). | Execute the desktop phases; keep pricing/FAQ copy on the current web + Local Mode promise until Phase 6 updates it. |

## Launch Blocker Policy

Status: Adopted

A launch blocker is a defect or missing requirement that would make the paid/public launch unsafe, unusable, or materially misleading. Use this policy before every triage pass.

Blocker categories:

| Category | Blocks Launch When | Examples |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Data loss | User-created project, manuscript, or worldbuilding content can disappear, overwrite incorrectly, or fail to persist without a clear recovery path. | Autosave loses recent writing; switching scenes overwrites text; deleting a project removes unrelated records. |
| Broken auth | Users cannot reliably sign up, sign in, sign out, restore sessions, or recover from common auth errors. | Fresh account cannot access the app; returning user gets stuck; expired session breaks navigation. |
| Broken save | Core user actions appear successful but do not persist after refresh, navigation, logout/login, or project switching. | Character edits vanish; project settings reset; chapter order is not saved. |
| Broken export | Promised export paths fail, omit critical content, corrupt ordering, or produce unusable files. | Manuscript export misses scenes; project archive cannot be opened; export crashes on realistic projects. |
| Unusable editor | The writing/editor workflow cannot support normal drafting without severe instability or blocked controls. | Cannot type reliably; cursor jumps constantly; scene selection breaks; long scenes make the editor unusable. |
| Unusable responsive layout | Core launch workflows cannot be completed on required mobile/tablet/desktop widths. | Save/export buttons overlap or disappear; editor cannot be used on mobile; modals trap users off-screen. |
| Missing legal/payment essentials | Purchase, account, pricing, privacy, terms, cancellation, or launch promise language is absent, broken, or materially misleading. | Paid plan promises unsupported features; checkout cannot complete; legal pages missing from purchase flow. |

Non-blockers:

- Cosmetic polish that does not prevent core workflows.
- Nice-to-have features without a launch promise.
- Advanced workflow refinements that are explicitly excluded from the final launch promise.
- Edge cases that have a clear workaround and do not risk data, payment, access, or legal trust.
- New feature ideas that have not yet been accepted into the final product promise.

Triage labels:

| Label | Meaning | Required Action |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Blocker | Fits one of the blocker categories above and prevents launch. | Fix before launch or change the launch promise/scope so it no longer blocks. |
| Conditional | Could block launch only if it is part of the paid/public promise or affects a required workflow. | Decide whether it is launch scope; otherwise mark Future/Excluded explicitly. |
| Launch Required | Required for the final paid launch product, even if beta can temporarily run without it. | Implement and QA before final paid/public launch. |
| Future/Excluded | Valuable but explicitly outside the final launch promise or a future business expansion. | Park with a clear product reason. |
| Icebox | Interesting, unvalidated, or not yet tied to a launch outcome. | Park until there is a stronger product reason. |

Triage rule:

Every bug or proposed launch change must answer both: does this create data loss, broken auth, broken save, broken export, unusable editor, unusable responsive layout, or missing legal/payment essentials; and is it part of the final product promise? Technical blockers decide beta safety, while final-product gaps decide final paid launch readiness.

Both fixes are theme-system-wide, not Quiet-Slate-specific, and were user-reported via screenshots of this build. Confirmed via `git show <commit> --stat` (pure-addition diff, nothing else touched) and a grep for any import referencing them (none) before deleting all 11 as dead duplicate artifacts — a concrete, observed instance of the exact shared-checkout collision risk the Agent Instructions' dev-server rule above warns about, this time hitting a merge rather than a dev server. Exports & Safety | Project export | Users can export a complete project archive containing manuscript and worldbuilding data in a restorable format. | Yes | QA passed 2026-07-22 — readable export expectations closed; category Word ZIP implemented 2026-08-08 | ZIP downloads for small/medium/large projects and was not corrupted. 2026-07-20/21: product decision made and shipped — the promised readable worldbuilding export is the DOCX bundle (`projectExportDocx.js`), not the World Bible PDF/HTML export; non-comic ZIP payload scoping fixed and unit-tested. 2026-07-22 live QA (real production account): exported "QA 13 - Novel" (81,962 words, 8 characters, 5 locations, 2 lore entries, 39 timeline events) as a Word document, captured the real download Blob via a monkey-patched `URL.createObjectURL`, parsed the DOCX's internal ZIP structure and inflated `word/document.xml` entirely in-browser (no external tools), and confirmed the resulting document contains the full manuscript prose in chapter order, the Story Outline, and real worldbuilding sections with actual data — a Characters section with named entries (name/role/age/bio, e.g. "Kael Morven — Role: Protagonist — Age: 42 — A senior warden from the capitol..."), Locations, Lore, Timeline, World History, and Factions. 2026-08-08 follow-up by product request: the user-facing Word export is now a single readable ZIP containing separate category `.docx` files instead of one combined document — Overview, Story Outline/Campaign Sessions/Comic Script, Characters, Locations, Factions, Lore, Notes, Timeline, World History, and AI Chats when those categories have content. Export-all-as-Word now flattens those category documents under one folder-like prefix per project inside the single all-projects ZIP, rather than nesting ZIPs. This closes the "export expectations for readable worldbuilding documents" item. Note: initial attempts at this live check hit a red herring — a long-lived browser tab from earlier in this session had a stale in-memory bundle referencing an old, no-longer-deployed JS chunk hash for the dynamically-imported `docx` library, causing "Word export failed: TypeError: Failed to fetch dynamically imported module" with zero user-facing feedback; a genuinely fresh tab/page load reproduced no such error, confirming this was a stale-session artifact and not a live production bug. While tracing it, found the root cause of why a stale/missing chunk fails this badly instead of cleanly: `vercel.json`'s catch-all SPA rewrite (`/(.*) → /index.html`) had no exclusion for `/assets/`, so any request for a chunk that doesn't exist (e.g. after a redeploy invalidates old hashes) silently gets served `index.html` (200 OK, `text/html`) instead of a real 404 — turning an ordinarily recoverable "stale chunk, please refresh" case into a confusing silent failure. Hardened the rewrite to `/((?!assets/).*) → /index.html` so missing/stale assets 404 properly; this is a defensive fix for future deploys, not a fix for a currently-reproducible bug. | Engineering/QA/Product |

Launch | Large project performance | Dashboard, manuscript, search, and export remain usable on a realistic large novel project. | Yes | QA passed 2026-07-22 | 2026-07-20: added `scripts/seed-stress-test.mjs`, a local-only generator (no live-account writes) that builds a project matching the checklist spec — 5 acts, 20 chapters, 80 scenes, exactly 80,000 words, 50 characters, 30 locations, 40 lore entries, 25 timeline events — as a localStorage-shaped JSON payload. 2026-07-22: loaded the generated payload into a running offline-mode dev build (temporarily served via a `public/_stress-test-data.json` fetch + localStorage injection, removed afterward) and exercised it end-to-end — dashboard project card/library snapshot, project Overview and Insights analytics, manuscript editor with full 80,000-word scroll and outline sidebar (5 acts/20 chapters/80 scenes all listed and clickable), scene-to-scene navigation via the outline, in-manuscript Search (12,301 matches across 80 scenes returned instantly with per-scene grouping), DOCX export trigger, the 50-entry Characters list and dossier view, the Relationship Map, and the 30-entry Locations list. No crashes, console errors, or visible freezing/lag at any point. Large-project performance is confirmed usable; closing this QA item. | Engineering/QA |
| 7. Launch | Local write path is capped far below the paid storage promise (browser-storage-full warning root cause) | Users must not see recurring "browser storage is full" alarms, and the local write path's real capacity must not fall dramatically short of what a paid plan promises (1GB trial / 5GB monthly / 8GB lifetime / 15GB founder in `PLAN_STORAGE_BYTES`, `src/utils/membership.js:11`). | Yes | Both phases implemented, unit-tested, and deployed 2026-07-27; live-account upload QA still needed | 2026-07-27 user report and root cause: see prior entry (browser localStorage hard-caps at ~5-10MB regardless of plan; every uploaded image was base64-encoded inline in the JSON written there). Implemented both phases: (1) `src/storage/indexedDbBackend.js` + `src/storage/browserVaultAdapter.js` — browser sessions now hydrate/write through IndexedDB instead of `localStorage`, mirroring the desktop vault's in-memory-mirror + async-write-behind contract exactly; wired into `src/main.jsx` boot alongside the existing desktop vault init, gated so desktop is untouched. (2) `src/utils/uploadUserMedia.js` — new shared upload/delete helper that optimises, quota-checks (wires up the previously-dead `checkUploadAllowed`), and uploads to a new public Supabase Storage bucket (`supabase/migrations/20260727_user_media_storage.sql`: bucket + owner-scoped write RLS + public-read RLS + a trigger that finally wires up the long-dormant `user_profiles.storage_used_bytes` column). Rewired all 8 image upload call sites (project/series covers and banners in `NovelManager.jsx`/`Layout.jsx`/`SeriesDashboard.jsx`, character portraits in `Characters.jsx`/`CharacterWizard.jsx`, faction logos in `LogoBuilder.jsx`, comic reference images in `ComicPlanner.jsx`) to upload instead of base64-encode, consolidating three duplicated inline canvas-resize functions into the shared helper; added best-effort Storage cleanup on image replace (including for draft/cancelable edit forms, tracked via a per-form pending-upload ref so an uploaded-but-never-saved image doesn't orphan) and on record deletion (`deleteNovel`/`deleteCharacter`/`deleteFaction`/`deleteSeries` in `useStore.js`). `useStore.js`'s `storageUsedBytes` is now media bytes (DB-authoritative, refetched on login/upload/delete) plus the existing local JSON-text estimate combined, replacing the old JSON-only estimate for both the Storage settings display and the pre-creation `storageExceededCheck()` gate — a bug in my own first pass (JSON-only) would have silently stopped enforcing the plan quota at all once images moved out of the JSON blob. Bucket is public-read and existing base64 images are lazily migrated (not backfilled) — both explicit user decisions. Testing: 14 new/updated unit tests (`indexedDbBackend.test.js`, `browserVaultAdapter.test.js` using `fake-indexeddb`, `uploadUserMedia.test.js`, plus `useStore.test.js` delete-cleanup cases), full suite 286/286 passing, production build clean. Live-verified in an offline-mode dev server: project create/persist through the new IndexedDB backend, and a synthetic cover-photo upload — which caught a real bug during this verification pass (see next sentence) and confirmed the fix. Found and fixed live: `uploadUserMedia.js` initially didn't check this codebase's `OFFLINE_MODE` flag (unlike every other Supabase-touching function in `firestoreSync.js`), so an offline-mode test session made one real HTTP request to the live production Supabase Storage API before failing cleanly on "Bucket not found" — no data was written (Storage uploads have no side effects until they succeed), but this was a real gap; fixed by adding the same `OFFLINE_MODE` guard used everywhere else, falling back to the pre-existing local data-URL behavior offline, with regression tests. Re-verified after the fix: zero network requests during the same offline-mode upload, image still worked locally via a `data:` URL. **2026-07-27 migration deployed**: with explicit user approval, applied both `20260706_desktop_devices.sql` (a previously-written, already-code-complete migration — matching `api/desktop-devices.js` — that had never actually been pushed to production, discovered as a side effect since `supabase db push` applies pending migrations in order) and `20260727_user_media_storage.sql` to the live "Your Own World" Supabase project (`cwifaklpjqutlcwvkxpp`) via `supabase db push`. Verified post-deploy via direct SQL query: `storage.buckets` has `user-media` with `public = true`, and all 4 RLS policies (`Public read user-media`, `Users upload/update/delete own user-media`) exist on `storage.objects`. **Remaining**: live-QA an actual signed-in upload end-to-end against the real account (real bucket write, RLS accepts the owner and rejects others, `user_profiles.storage_used_bytes` increments/decrements correctly on upload/delete, and a replaced/deleted image's old Storage object is actually removed) before considering this fully closed — not done this pass since it needs a real authenticated browser session, not the offline dev server used for the rest of this pass's verification. | Product/Engineering |

Final paid launch, however, means the fully specced website: each project type needs its own structure, language, defaults, export expectations, QA scenarios, and promised discipline-specific workflow. ## PRD: Comic Panel Engine

### Product Summary

The Comic Panel Engine turns Comic/Graphic Novel projects from a renamed prose outline into a sequential-art planning workspace. It must let writers plan volumes, issues, pages, and panels; capture the writing and art-direction details needed by a solo creator or writer/artist team; and export a readable comic script and project backup without data loss.

This is final-launch scope for Comic/Graphic Novel. Until this engine is implemented and QA-passed, Comic/Graphic Novel remains beta/limited in product copy.

### Problem

The current Comic/Graphic Novel workflow supports comic-specific project type labels and templates, but page planning still depends on the shared manuscript scene model. That is not enough for comics because creators need to think in pages, panel counts, panel beats, visual composition, captions, speech balloons, sound effects, and page-turn reveals. Without a real page/panel model, YOW risks over-promising a format-specific workflow that cannot support normal comic scripting.

### Target Users

- Writer planning a comic issue or graphic novel script before handing it to an artist.
- Writer-artist thumbnailing page beats and visual direction before drafting final pages.
- Editor or collaborator reviewing whether a page has too many panels, unclear dialogue, missing art notes, or weak page turns.

### Goals

- Provide a comic-native structure: Volume → Issue → Page → Panel.
- Let users create, reorder, duplicate, edit, and delete pages and panels without losing linked content.
- Capture page-level intent: page title, summary, status, page type, page-turn intent, location/time, characters, visual notes, and production notes.
- Capture panel-level script detail: panel number, shot/framing, description, art direction, characters, dialogue, captions, SFX, lettering notes, continuity notes, and panel status.
- Give users a fast page-planning view that shows page count, panel count, dialogue density, status, and page-turn beats.
- Export comic projects in a readable script format and include panel data in ZIP backup/restore.
- Keep existing prose, campaign, and worldbuilding workflows unaffected.

### Non-Goals

- Finished comic page layout, drawing, image generation, lettering, or print prepress.
- Real-time collaboration or artist assignment workflows.
- Advanced thumbnail canvas editing.
- Branching versions of the same panel/page.
- Replacing existing character, location, lore, map, or timeline systems.

### Core User Stories

- As a comic writer, I can add pages inside an issue and panels inside each page so I can plan a complete issue.
- As a comic writer, I can enter dialogue, captions, SFX, and art notes per panel so my script is useful to an artist.
- As a writer-artist, I can set page-level visual direction and page-turn purpose so each page has a clear storytelling job.
- As an editor, I can scan pages for panel count, status, and dialogue load so I can spot pacing problems quickly.
- As a user, I can refresh, navigate away, export, restore, and log back in without losing page or panel work.

### Data Model Requirements

Reuse the existing project structure where practical: Volume maps to the existing first structural level and Issue maps to the second structural level. Add comic-specific records for pages and panels rather than overloading prose scene content.

Comic page record:

| Field | Requirement |
| --- | --- |
| `id` | Stable page ID for local state, cloud sync, export, and restore. |
| `novelId` | Project ID. Keep the existing naming convention unless the store is renamed globally. |
| `volumeId` | Parent Volume/act ID. |
| `issueId` | Parent Issue/chapter ID. |
| `order` | Numeric page order within the issue. |
| `title` | Optional page title or label. |
| `summary` | Page-level story beat. |
| `pageNumber` | Display page number, auto-derived by default and manually overrideable if needed. |
| `pageType` | Standard, splash, double-page spread, cover, backmatter, recap, credits, or custom. |
| `status` | Outline, Draft, Revised, Final. |
| `pageTurn` | None, reveal, cliffhanger, joke, emotional beat, action beat, or custom. |
| `locationIds` | Linked locations. |
| `characterIds` | Linked characters appearing on the page. |
| `timeOfDay` | Optional continuity field. |
| `visualDirection` | Page-level composition, mood, palette, reference, or style notes. |
| `productionNotes` | Editor/artist/letterer notes. |
| `createdAt` / `updatedAt` | Persistence and conflict visibility. |

Comic panel record:

| Field | Requirement |
| --- | --- |
| `id` | Stable panel ID. |
| `novelId` | Project ID. |
| `pageId` | Parent comic page ID. |
| `order` | Numeric panel order within the page. |
| `panelNumber` | Display number, auto-derived from order. |
| `layoutHint` | Full width, tall, wide, inset, borderless, close-up, establishing, action, reaction, or custom. |
| `shotType` | Establishing, wide, medium, close-up, extreme close-up, POV, over-shoulder, detail, or custom. |
| `description` | What the reader sees. |
| `artNotes` | Direction for composition, motion, expression, props, continuity, references. |
| `dialogue` | Ordered balloon lines with speaker, text, and optional emotion/lettering note. |
| `captions` | Ordered caption boxes with text and optional type: narration, location, time, thought, editorial. |
| `sfx` | Ordered sound effects with text and optional placement/style note. |
| `characterIds` | Characters visible or speaking in the panel. |
| `locationIds` | Optional panel-specific location override. |
| `continuityNotes` | Prop/costume/time continuity. |
| `status` | Outline, Draft, Revised, Final. |
| `createdAt` / `updatedAt` | Persistence and conflict visibility. |

### UX Requirements

- Comic projects show a Comic Planning workspace or comic-specific mode inside Manuscript.
- The left structure keeps Volume and Issue navigation.
- The main area shows a page list for the selected issue with compact status, panel count, page type, page-turn marker, and last updated time.
- Selecting a page opens a page editor with page-level fields and a sortable panel list.
- Panel editor supports fast creation of multiple panels, duplication, reordering, deletion, and collapse/expand.
- Dialogue, captions, and SFX are separate repeatable rows, not one undifferentiated text box.
- Page and panel records autosave with the same reliability expectations as manuscript scenes.
- Empty states are comic-specific: first page, first panel, and empty issue states should guide the user into page planning without marketing copy.
- Mobile/tablet layout must preserve page selection, panel editing, save state, and export access without overlapping controls.

### Export Requirements

ZIP backup/restore:

- Include comic pages and panels in project export JSON.
- Restore must remap page and panel IDs, preserve parent relationships, preserve linked characters/locations where possible, and avoid collisions with existing project IDs.
- Restore must not drop unknown future comic fields.

DOCX/PDF export:

- Comic projects export as a readable comic script organized by Volume, Issue, Page, and Panel.
- Each page includes page metadata, summary, page-turn intent, visual direction, and production notes when present.
- Each panel includes panel description, art notes, dialogue, captions, SFX, continuity notes, and linked character/location names where present.
- Export should include page and panel counts in issue summaries.
- Empty pages or panels should export with clear placeholders rather than failing.

### AI Requirements

- AI prompt context for Comic/Graphic Novel should include project type, issue/page/panel structure, current page summary, panel count, selected panel text, linked characters, and linked locations when available.
- AI actions may suggest panel beats, dialogue trims, page-turn ideas, or visual direction.
- AI must not overwrite page or panel content without explicit user action.

### Acceptance Criteria

- A user can create a Comic/Graphic Novel project, add at least one Volume, Issue, Page, and Panel, refresh immediately, and all content remains.
- A user can reorder pages within an issue and panels within a page; order persists after refresh and export/restore.
- A user can duplicate a page with all panels and then edit the duplicate without changing the original.
- Deleting a page deletes or safely detaches its panels with a clear confirmation.
- Deleting a linked character or location removes or labels broken comic links consistently with existing worldbuilding cleanup behavior.
- Comic page and panel data is included in local storage, cloud save, ZIP export, ZIP restore, DOCX export, and PDF export.
- Comic export remains readable when an issue has no pages, a page has no panels, or a panel has only notes and no dialogue.
- Existing Novel, Novella, Short Story, D&D Campaign, and TTRPG Campaign projects continue to create, edit, save, and export without comic fields appearing in their core workflows.

### QA Plan

**1. Structure CRUD**
- Create a Comic/Graphic Novel project; confirm Comic Planner opens instead of prose Manuscript.
- Add a Volume; confirm it appears in the sidebar.
- Add an Issue under the Volume; confirm it appears nested and is selectable.
- Double-click Volume and Issue titles to rename; confirm names save on blur/Enter.
- Delete an Issue that has pages; confirm pages are removed and sidebar updates.
- Delete a Volume that has issues; confirm all child issues and their pages are removed.

**2. Page CRUD**
- Select an Issue; add at least 3 pages; confirm they appear in the page list with ascending numbers.
- Edit page title, page type, status, page turn, summary, visual direction, production notes; refresh and confirm all fields persist.
- Duplicate a page; confirm the duplicate has the same fields and panels; edit the duplicate and confirm the original is unchanged.
- Delete a page that has panels; confirm the panels are removed and the list updates.

**3. Panel CRUD**
- Add 4 panels to a page; confirm they each expand and collapse independently.
- Fill in description, art notes, shot type, layout, status, continuity notes; add dialogue balloons (with speaker), captions (with type), and SFX rows; refresh and confirm all fields persist.
- Delete a panel; confirm it is removed and remaining panels are renumbered.
- Link and unlink characters from page and panel character chips; refresh and confirm the selection persists.

**4. Reference image upload (page and panel level)**
- Upload a PNG or JPG to a page reference; confirm it appears as a thumbnail; refresh and confirm the image persists.
- Upload a WebP or GIF; confirm it processes and saves correctly.
- Upload a PDF; confirm the iframe preview appears; confirm the "Session only — not saved on refresh" badge is visible; refresh and confirm the PDF preview is gone (session-only confirmed).
- Click Replace on an existing image and upload a different image; confirm the old image is gone.
- Click Remove; confirm the image is cleared and the drop zone returns.
- Repeat upload tests at the panel level.

**5. Persistence**
- Fill page and panel fields, add a page reference image, then immediately refresh; confirm all content and the image remain.
- Navigate to another project section (worldbuilding, etc.) and back to Manuscript; confirm the comic planner state is preserved.
- Sign out and sign back in; confirm all pages, panels, fields, and reference images are restored from cloud save.

**6. Export — ZIP round-trip**
- Export a comic project ZIP; open the ZIP and confirm `data/comic-pages.json` and `data/comic-panels.json` are present with correct content.
- Import the ZIP as a new project; confirm all volumes, issues, pages, panels, text fields, and reference images are restored under a new project entry.

**7. Export — DOCX**
- Export DOCX for a comic project; confirm a "Comic Script" section appears.
- Confirm Volume and Issue headings are present; confirm each page shows page number, metadata, summary, and visual direction.
- Confirm each panel shows description, art notes, dialogue balloons, captions, SFX, and continuity notes.
- Export a comic with an empty issue (no pages) and a page with no panels; confirm the export does not crash and renders clear placeholders.

**8. Non-comic project isolation**
- Open a Novel, Novella, Short Story, D&D Campaign, or TTRPG Campaign project; confirm the prose Manuscript opens normally with no comic fields visible.
- Export a non-comic project DOCX; confirm no comic script section appears.

**9. Responsive**
- Test at 375px: confirm sidebar, page list, and panel editor are accessible without overlapping controls; confirm the drop zone and image preview are usable.
- Test at 768px: confirm the three-panel layout adjusts correctly.
- Test at 1280px+: confirm the layout uses available space without clipping.

**10. Data safety**
- Delete a character that is linked to pages and panels; confirm the character is removed from `characterIds` on affected records or appears as a broken link consistently with existing worldbuilding cleanup behaviour.
- Restore a ZIP into an account that already has other projects; confirm existing projects are unaffected.
- Create a comic project, add pages and panels, then delete the entire project; confirm all comic page and panel records are removed from storage.

### Implementation Notes

- Prefer additive store fields such as `comicPages` and `comicPanels` over mutating the prose scene model into panel data.
- Keep comic page/panel records project-scoped and compatible with current local-first save and Supabase JSON persistence.
- Use existing project export/import ID-remapping utilities where possible.
- Gate comic-specific UI by `project.type === 'comic'`.
- Keep roadmap status as beta/limited until CRUD, persistence, export, restore, and responsive QA pass.

### Open Decisions

- Whether double-page spreads are modeled as one page record with `pageType: double-page spread` or as linked left/right page records.
- Whether page numbering should be per issue only or continuous across a graphic novel.
- Whether panel dialogue needs rich formatting in the first release or plain structured rows are sufficient.
- Whether visual reference images belong in this engine now or should remain in existing image/map/lore systems until a future product decision.

## All Project Types Live Plan

Operating model: use beta to ship breadth safely, then deepen each discipline before final launch. A beta project type is allowed to be selectable if the UI labels it honestly, data persists safely, export does not crash, and missing workflow-specific features are not advertised as complete. Final launch requires the workflow waves below to be complete enough that the site can stand without planned continual development.

Current status: Wave 0 is implemented and partially smoke-tested. Wave 1 shared type foundation is implemented for type defaults, section defaults on new projects, starter outlines, dashboard language, type-specific workflow summaries, AI prompt context, workspace badges, project-settings type notes, and generic export labels; automated config/export smoke passed across all 6 active types. Wave 2 tabletop session engine adds structured session prep and recap fields to existing campaign session records and includes them in campaign exports; browser QA is still needed. Wave 3 comic panel engine implemented 2026-06-13 with Volume → Issue → Page → Panel structure, full page/panel field editors, reference image/PDF upload, DOCX script export, and ZIP round-trip; browser QA needed.

| Wave | Scope | Acceptance Criteria | QA Gate |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 0. Beta access | All 6 active project types can be created and edited; beta types show limited-workflow notes. | Create/edit flow accepts all configured project type IDs; project header shows type badge; homepage avoids unsupported feature claims. | Smoke check every type: create, open, add one structure item, refresh, export ZIP. |
| 1. Shared type foundation | Project-type defaults, dashboard language, AI prompt context, export labels, section defaults, and workspace badges. | Each type has distinct structure labels, default sections, prompt guidance, and visible type identity. | Smoke check live/beta type language in dashboard, manuscript, AI tools, and export menu. |
| 2. Tabletop session engine | D&D/TTRPG get structured session planning and recap fields without replacing existing campaign bible data. | User can plan and recap a session with hooks, encounters, NPCs, rewards, and consequences. | Deep QA campaign planning, persistence, and export. |
| 3. Comic panel engine | Comic/Graphic Novel gets page/panel planning, visual direction fields, reference image/PDF upload, and comic script export. | User can outline pages, panels, captions/dialogue, art notes, and upload reference images; data round-trips through ZIP and DOCX export. | Deep QA per the QA Plan in the Comic Panel Engine PRD above. |

These items can ship in beta only if they are already stable, required by a launch promise, or needed to avoid a blocker. For final paid/public launch, any desired product capability must be complete or explicitly moved to Future/Excluded by product decision.

| 3. Core Polish | Modals and overlays | All critical modals open, close, trap focus reasonably, preserve unsaved work, and use consistent spacing and button order. | Conditional | Regression fixed 2026-08-01 — needs a fresh mobile QA pass | 2026-07-19 manual QA passed project edit modal, dirty discard prompt, focus trap, modal spacing/order, mobile modal fit, and beta disclaimer behavior. 2026-07-22 investigation (real production account) resolved the "series edit unclear" note: a full Edit Series modal does exist (cover photo, name, summary, status, tags, Shared World-Building toggles, delete series), but was only reachable via a `.series-dash-card-hover` wrapper (`opacity: 0; pointer-events: none` at rest, no persistent icon), unlike sibling project cards showing always-visible gear/download/star icons — undiscoverable at rest and unreachable on touch (no hover state). 2026-07-23 fix: moved the series card's edit action out of the hover-reveal panel into a persistent, always-visible icon button on the cover (`SeriesCard` in `NovelManager.jsx`, reusing the existing `.dash-card-settings-button` styling already used by project cards), removed the now-dead `.series-dash-card-edit-btn` hover-only styles. Live-verified against a local dev server on the real account at desktop and 375px mobile widths: the edit icon is visible without hovering and opens the correct series' Edit modal; confirmed reachable by tap at mobile width. 2026-07-23: also fixed the click-outside-to-close inconsistency (see "Save and close behavior" row for the root cause and fix) — the shared `StudioSheet` editor modal's backdrop was missing an `onClick` handler entirely; now wired to the same guarded close used by Escape/Cancel/×. Both discoverability and click-outside gaps are closed. 2026-08-01 user report ("mobile UI is horrendous, text overlap, modals don't work") root-caused via live mobile-viewport reproduction (offline dev server, 375px): found a leftover, unconditional debug diagnostic in `StudioSheet` (`src/components/presentation/Studio.jsx`) — a `TEMPORARY diagnostic — remove once the phone-modal-clipping bug is found` block from an earlier investigation that was never removed. On any phone-width viewport (`isPhone`, ≤640px) it rendered a fixed, full-width, bright-magenta monospace debug bar (`innerWH=… vv=… scrollY=… pos=… inset=… rect: …`) portaled to `document.body` at `z-index: 999999`, sitting on top of every modal that uses the shared `StudioSheet`/`Modal.jsx` wrapper (Characters, Locations, Lore, World History, Factions logo editor, and any other consumer of `shared/Modal.jsx`) — this covered the modal header/title on phones and is almost certainly what read as "modals don't work." Fixed by deleting the diagnostic state/effect and its portal render entirely; live-verified at 375px that the Edit Character modal now opens with a clean header and no overlay. Same pass also fixed two other confirmed mobile-only defects found in the same sweep: (1) `.active-project-command-stats--compact .active-project-command-stat` (Library Snapshot's Projects/Series/Total words tiles) had a `min-width: 130px` rule whose selector specificity (two classes) beat the existing mobile `min-width: 0` override (one class) despite coming later in the file, causing the 3-tile row to overflow its container by ~32px and clip the third tile at ≤640px — fixed by adding a matching compound-selector override inside the existing `@media (max-width: 640px)` block in `src/index.css`. (2) The library top bar's "Import ▾" dropdown menu (`NovelManager.jsx`) was positioned `left: 0` relative to the Import button, which sits near the right edge of a narrow viewport — the 196px-wide menu overflowed the right edge of the screen by ~10px at 375px; fixed by anchoring it `right: 0` instead (opens leftward, matching the button's actual position) with a `maxWidth: calc(100vw - 32px)` safety clamp. All three fixes verified live at 375px; `npm run lint` and `npm run build` both clean, no new warnings. This was a spot-check across the library dashboard, project overview, Characters (list, dossier, edit modal), Family Tree, AI Import modal, and Account Settings (Profile/Appearance tabs) — not an exhaustive re-sweep of every screen the 2026-07 passes covered, so re-run a fresh mobile QA pass on the remaining worldbuilding sections (Locations, Lore, Timeline/History, Factions, Map, Schedule) before closing this row again, since they share the same `StudioSheet` modal and are equally likely to have been hit by the same debug-bar regression. 2026-08-01 follow-up (user-supplied real-device screenshots against production, taken after the fixes above but before any deploy — production still has none of this branch's changes): two more concrete bugs confirmed live and fixed. (1) Library top bar button overlap: `.library-top-bar .user-menu-trigger-name`/`-caret` only collapsed to avatar-only at `≤420px`, but the New Project/New Series/Import row's mobile shrink range is `≤640px` and its buttons use `white-space: nowrap` with no `overflow:hidden` — on wider phones (421-640px, e.g. Pro Max-class ~428-430px) with the account trigger showing a full name plus the AI-chat and tour "?" icon buttons, the shrunk button pills' text overflowed their own box and visually overlapped the next pill ("NEW PROJEEW SERIESMPORT" in the user's screenshot). Fixed by moving the avatar-only collapse into the same `≤640px` block (so it always coincides with the button-shrink range) and adding `overflow:hidden; text-overflow:ellipsis` to the buttons as a defensive fallback. (2) Double-height in-project mobile header, reported directly ("put the hamburger menu icon in the actual top bar so there doesn't have to be a double height top bar"): `.studio-room-hamburger` (`src/components/presentation/Studio.jsx` / `index.css`) was forced onto its own full-width `grid-row: 2` below the brand/utility/account row at `≤860px`, costing ~44px of vertical space on every in-project screen. Fixed by moving it into row 1's existing (previously unused-on-mobile) third grid column instead of forcing a second row — appended as a final `@media (max-width: 860px)` override (same last-in-cascade convention used elsewhere in this file) since `.studio-spine` has several earlier, mutually inconsistent grid definitions at this breakpoint. Doing this shrank the column available to the Write-button-plus-Settings-gear-plus-Exit-chevron cluster (which share a column with no wrap protection) enough to reproduce a second, smaller live-confirmed overlap — the Settings gear icon bleeding ~9px into the Write button — fixed by also collapsing the in-project account trigger (`.studio-spine .user-menu-trigger-name`/`-caret`) to avatar-only at the same `≤860px` breakpoint, freeing enough width. All four fixes (this row's two plus the two above) verified live at 375/390/430px with zero detected bounding-box overlaps via a scripted sibling-overlap check, and confirmed unaffected at 768px/1280px (hamburger and account name both correctly still show full desktop treatment above 860px). Still unresolved: the user's second screenshot also showed the Edit Character modal's own header (kicker/title/×) missing entirely on a real device, with the page's section-tab strip appearing to render directly above the form fields with no gap — this was NOT reproducible in this sandboxed dev-server session (the same modal/tab combination renders its header correctly here after the debug-bar fix above), and the screenshot's account ("Business Email", 14 projects, real project data) confirms it was taken against production, which has none of this branch's fixes yet — so it cannot be distinguished from the already-fixed debug-bar bug without a real-device retest against a deployed build. `npm run lint` and `npm run build` clean throughout, no new warnings. 2026-08-01 second follow-up (user: "now the top bar is just super thick, make it shorter" — after the single-row merge above): the merge fixed the layout but not the height — `.studio-spine` still measured 90px tall as one row, same as the old two-row total, because the file's several earlier `.studio-spine` blocks at this breakpoint each hardcode their own `min-height` (58px/90px/96px/102px depending on which block wins) sized for the old two-row content, and none of those get touched by overriding `grid-template-rows` alone since `min-height` is a separate property. Fixed by adding `min-height: 0; height: auto;` to the same appended override, letting the row size to its actual content instead of an inherited two-row constraint — live-verified the bar dropped from 90px to 47px at 390px width with zero new overlaps (rechecked via the same scripted bounding-box sweep) and confirmed still unaffected above 860px. 2026-08-01 third follow-up (user: explicit reorder request — "Characters and hamburger should be farthest to the left. settings and back to dashboard next to character icon. YoW logo in the middle, Write as part of the hamburger menu"): rebuilt the ≤860px mobile header's column model from scratch instead of continuing to patch individual cell assignments (`src/index.css`, same appended override block) — `grid-template-columns: max-content max-content minmax(0, 1fr) max-content` with hamburger in col 1, the Settings-gear/Exit-chevron utility cluster in col 2 (right next to it, `justify-content: flex-start`), the YOW brand mark centred in col 3 (`minmax(0,1fr)`, `justify-content: center`), and the account avatar in col 4. The standalone mobile "Write" button (`.studio-mobile-primary-action`) is now hidden entirely at this breakpoint. `StudioFrame` (`src/components/presentation/Studio.jsx`) gained a new first entry in the hamburger's dropdown (`studio-room-menu-portal`) that renders a Write row (pencil icon, "Write — Open the manuscript", separated by a border from the section list below) and calls the existing `primaryAction` element's own `onClick` reflectively (`primaryAction.props.onClick`) rather than plumbing a new prop through Layout.jsx, since this is a single-consumer component already tightly coupled to it. Live-verified at 375/390px: hamburger+Settings+Exit sit flush left, the YOW mark is centred in the remaining space, the avatar is far right, tapping "Write" at the top of the hamburger menu closes the menu and correctly opens the manuscript editor (confirmed via the Chapter 1 "Begin writing here…" editor appearing), and the room list below Write still navigates normally. Zero overlaps via the same scripted sibling bounding-box sweep. Confirmed unaffected at 768px/1280px (desktop keeps its own unrelated top bar with Write in its normal spot next to the logo). `npm run lint` and `npm run build` clean, same pre-existing warnings only. 2026-08-01 fourth follow-up (user: "put the settings, back, and tours buttons next to the user account icon. let's put the logo back, and make the logo act as the hamburger menu"): reworked the same ≤860px column model again — the logo-hamburger (below) now covers the brand mark's only mobile job, so `.studio-brand` is hidden outright rather than centred; `.studio-utility` (Settings/Exit/tour "?", all rendered together already) moves from beside the hamburger to beside the account avatar on the far right (`grid-template-columns: max-content minmax(0,1fr) max-content max-content` — hamburger, flexible spacer, utility, account); and in `Studio.jsx` the hamburger button's icon changed from the plain ☰/✕ glyph (`RoomMenuIcon`, now unused and deleted) to `<YOWLogo />`, so tapping the logo itself opens/closes the section menu instead of a separate hamburger glyph — the room-name label next to it is unchanged. Live-verified at 375px: logo+"Overview" sits far left, Settings/Exit sit immediately left of the avatar on the far right, the middle is empty flexible space, tapping the logo opens the same section menu (Write/Overview/Planning/Characters/Atlas/Lore/AI Tools) as before, bar height 35px. Zero overlaps via the scripted sweep; unaffected at 768px/1280px (desktop's `.studio-brand` and room-list are untouched, this override is scoped ≤860px only). `npm run lint`/`npm run build` clean (no new warnings from the `RoomMenuIcon` removal). 2026-08-01 fifth follow-up (user: "on the projects dashboard, make the add project, add series, and import buttons into icons so they can be small enough to fit in the top bar"): the library dashboard's New Project/New Series/Import buttons (`NovelManager.jsx`) previously wrapped onto their own full-width row below the logo at ≤640px (`.library-action-cluster { grid-column: 1 / -1 }` inside a single-column `.library-top-bar-left` grid). Added an SVG icon (file-plus / stacked-books / download-into-tray) plus `aria-label`/`title` to each button, hidden by default (`.library-action-icon { display: none }`, desktop/tablet unchanged) and shown icon-only at ≤640px via a new appended override that also switches `.library-top-bar-left` and `.library-action-cluster` from the old grid-stacking model to a single flex row, and shrinks the three buttons to 30×30px icon-only circles (label hidden, `font-size: 0`). Live-verified at 375px: logo, Beta badge, and all three icon buttons now share one row with the AI-chat/account cluster, accessible names are preserved (confirmed via the accessibility tree: "New Project"/"New Series"/"Import" survive as the buttons' names even with visible text hidden), the Import dropdown still opens correctly positioned under its now-smaller icon. Confirmed unaffected at 1280px (desktop keeps the original full-text pill buttons). `npm run lint`/`npm run build` clean. 2026-08-01 sixth follow-up (user: "dashboard is side scrolling and the project is stuck with part being cut off... i think it happened after i clicked to add a series"): could not reproduce via automated overflow scans in this sandbox (Chromium doesn't have the WebKit behavior involved), but the repro steps and symptom (page appears panned/cut off, persisting across in-app navigation to an unrelated screen) match a well-known iOS Safari bug: focusing a text input with `font-size` under 16px makes Safari auto-zoom the whole page to make the text legible, and that zoom/pan can be left stuck after the input blurs, especially across an SPA route change that doesn't reset it. `NovelManager.jsx`'s New Series form's `autoFocus` input — which grabs focus the instant the "New Series" button is tapped, exactly matching "after I clicked to add a series" — was `fontSize: 14`. Audited all four related forms sharing this code (New Project quick-create, `EditProjectModal`/Project Settings, New Series quick-create, `EditSeriesModal`) and found the same sub-16px pattern on every text input/textarea/select a user can type into (14px on the four `autoFocus` name/title fields, 13px on the three summary/description text areas and the series `<select>`) — bumped all seven to 16px, matching the standard web mitigation for this exact bug (`user-scalable=no` is not used and should not be added, since that's an accessibility anti-pattern; fixing the trigger font-size preserves manual pinch-zoom). Not verified against real iOS Safari (only available via Chromium here) — needs a real-device retest of the New Series flow specifically, and ideally the other three forms too, once deployed. Flagged as a background task: the same sub-16px input pattern likely exists much more broadly across the app via the shared `.field` class combined with Tailwind's `text-sm` (14px) utility, used in Characters/Locations/Lore/etc. — out of scope to rewrite here without a specific report, but worth a dedicated sweep. `npm run lint`/`npm run build` clean. 2026-08-01 seventh follow-up (user, testing via Xcode + iPhone 17 Pro Max Simulator against local builds, not production — clarifying the earlier "which environment" question): confirmed the debug-bar fix, font-size fix, and header layout changes above did not resolve the edit-modal report; user asked to stop patching the fixed-position overlay and instead have editors "open to another part of the page" on mobile. Root-caused as likely inherent to the fixed-overlay pattern itself: `StudioSheet` on phones (`isPhone`) was `position: fixed; inset: 0`, and while every specific trigger checked out clean in this sandbox (no `transform`/`filter`-bearing ancestor breaking the fixed containing block, no `-webkit-overflow-scrolling: touch` ancestor in this component tree, dialog `.focus()` already skipped on phones, input font-sizes already fixed), real iOS Safari has enough different fixed-position-plus-viewport-resize edge cases (webkit.org/b/236584 and others) that a Chromium-only sandbox can't fully rule them out, and the report persisted across three separate legitimate fixes. Rebuilt per the explicit request instead of continuing to patch the overlay: `StudioSheet` (`src/components/presentation/Studio.jsx`) no longer applies `position: fixed` (or any `position: absolute`) on phones at all — `.studio-sheet-backdrop.is-mobile-sheet`/`.studio-sheet.is-mobile-sheet` (`src/index.css`) are now `position: static`, full-width, `min-height: 100dvh`, rendered as an ordinary in-flow block exactly where the `{showForm && <Modal>}` JSX already sits in the DOM (right after the room's normal content). On open, a `useEffect` calls `dialogRef.current.scrollIntoView({ block: 'start' })` instead of the previous `.focus()` call, so it reads as navigating to a new part of the page; closing it removes the block and the page naturally lands back on the underlying list/detail view. Backdrop click-to-close is disabled on phones (`closeOnBackdrop && !isPhone`) since there's no visible "outside" to tap once it's not an overlay. Live-verified at 375px: opening Edit Character scrolls smoothly to a normal (non-overlay) full-height editor card with its own working header/×, scrolling up reveals the original Characters list/dossier still in place above it, closing lands back on that view, zero page-level horizontal overflow (only the pre-existing, intentionally-scrollable sub-tab strip), and desktop (1280px) is completely unaffected — same centered fixed-position dialog as before, since this all sits behind the `isPhone` check. Same follow-up also removed the centred YOW brand mark from the mobile in-project header per explicit request ("just remove the logo") — hamburger-plus-room-name stays far left, Settings/Exit/tour stay next to the account avatar on the right, nothing occupies the middle now. `npm run lint`/`npm run build` clean, same pre-existing warnings only. This is a genuine architecture change for every `StudioSheet`/`Modal.jsx` consumer (Characters, Locations, Lore, World History, Factions, and any future one) — still needs a real-device retest on the reporting user's actual Simulator setup, since it could not be verified against real WebKit from this sandbox. 2026-08-02 eighth follow-up (user: "need to be able to minimise the menus. when editing it should populate above the character info tabs"): on phones, editing the currently-open character still required scrolling past the full read-only dossier (Overview/Journey/Relationships/Character Traits/Background tabs and their content) to reach the in-flow editor appended at the very end of `Characters.jsx`. Restructured so editing the character that's already open (`editTarget.id === selected.id`, i.e. the "Edit" button on an open dossier, not "New"/"Add Character") swaps the read-only tabs and their content out for the editor in place, right after the name/portrait header — the editor now appears exactly where the tabs used to be instead of below a potentially long dossier, and since the tabs are replaced rather than just covered, there's nothing extra to scroll past or minimize. Creating a brand-new character (no dossier open, or "New" clicked) is unaffected — same fallback editor placement as before. Live-verified at 375px: tapping Edit on "Test Hero" now shows the editor's header immediately below the "Browse list" toggle (scrollTop stayed at ~38px, total page height dropped from over 2500px to ~707px), closing returns cleanly to the read-only tabs, and desktop (1280px) renders identically to before (same centered fixed dialog — this only changes *where in the DOM* the editor renders, which doesn't affect the desktop's fixed-position presentation). `npm run lint`/`npm run build` clean. This batch of fixes (debug-bar removal through the eighth follow-up) was committed and pushed to `main` (04fe14e) on 2026-08-01 and confirmed live on production via a real-device screenshot (real iPhone Safari, yourownworld.co.uk, a "Brannic Sol" character dossier): the in-project header renders as the single-row hamburger/Settings/Exit/avatar layout, and opening Edit now shows a correctly-headered "EDITOR / Edit Brannic Sol / ×" block in place — the modal-header bug that persisted across four earlier fix attempts is confirmed resolved on real WebKit. 2026-08-02 ninth follow-up, from that same real-device screenshot plus a fresh report: (1) "Improve button layout on character view" — the dossier's Edit/Delete buttons were floating dead-centre under the portrait with large empty gaps on both sides. Root cause: `.studio-page-actions { align-self: center }`, a mobile rule written for single-button action bars elsewhere (workspace header, index tools — see its own comment, "keep at natural width instead of forcing full row"), was also matching this dossier header's actions block; `.studio-page-header` already stacks to a left-aligned column on phones, so without that stray override the buttons would sit flush left under the title/chips on their own. Fixed with a final appended `align-self: flex-start` override for `.studio-page-actions` specifically. (2) "Status Queue gets stuck on bottom of the page and cannot be scrolled": the library dashboard's root scroll container (`NovelManager.jsx`) had `-webkit-overflow-scrolling: touch` — the same legacy iOS momentum-scroll property already identified as the likely cause of the New Series "stuck zoomed" bug earlier in this row. On real iOS Safari this property is known to cause a scrollable container's height to stop recalculating correctly after its content grows (e.g. as project/status data loads in after first paint), leaving the last section (Status Queue, at the very bottom) effectively unreachable — content the container "thinks" doesn't exist. Modern iOS (13+) has native momentum scrolling on any `overflow: auto` element and no longer needs this legacy property. Removed it, and swapped the container's `height: 100vh` for `100dvh` to match this codebase's established mobile-safe-area convention. Both fixes verified live at 375px (button alignment) and unaffected at 1280px (desktop identical to before in both cases); `npm run lint` (0 errors, pre-existing warning count unchanged), `npx vitest run` (308/308 passing), and `npm run build` all clean. Neither the Status Queue nor the WebKit-only zoom bug could be reproduced directly in this Chromium sandbox — both fixes are reasoned from known WebKit behavior matching the reported symptoms exactly, not from a local repro, so still worth a real-device confirmation once deployed. | Engineering/Design |

Exports & Safety | Storage usage tracking | Users can see meaningful storage usage or limits before hitting failure states. | Conditional | QA passed 2026-07-22 — cloud cap edge cases closed | 2026-07-19 manual QA confirmed the storage card shows meaningful realistic usage. 2026-07-22 live QA (real production account, Monthly plan, 507 KB of 5 GB used) used the app's built-in `localStorage.__yow_storage_test` dev hook (forces `storageQuotaBytes` to 1 byte, see `src/App.jsx`) to exercise the otherwise-unreachable over-cap path without filling 5 GB of real data: confirmed new-record writes are correctly blocked (attempted to save a new character — the save was silently rejected and no character was created), confirmed the "Cloud storage limit reached…" toast renders with the correct copy and used/quota figures (via `membership-read-only` event with `reason: 'storage-exceeded'`), and confirmed its "Storage settings" button correctly deep-links to Account Settings → Membership. Flag cleared afterward. 2026-07-22 bug found and fixed: the single-file upload gate (`MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES`/`checkUploadAllowed` in `src/utils/storageQuota.js`, and the shared `optimizeImageToDataUrl` resizer in `src/utils/imageOptimize.js`) was correctly wired for project cover/banner photos, faction logos, RPG character portraits, and the account avatar, but two upload sites had no size gate at all — raw `FileReader.readAsDataURL` with zero compression or rejection: character portraits (`Characters.jsx`) and series cover photos (`SeriesDashboard.jsx`). A large enough upload at either of those two sites would have silently written an arbitrarily large base64 blob with no "file too large" feedback. Fixed both to use `optimizeImageToDataUrl` (matching the pattern already used elsewhere) with a visible error message on rejection; live-verified in an offline dev build that a 61.3 MB test image is now correctly rejected with "Image is too large (61.3 MB). Maximum accepted size is 15 MB." with no crash, and that a normal-sized image still uploads and displays correctly (regression check passed). 2026-08-05 bug found and fixed: the 2026-07-22 pass above confirmed writes were correctly *blocked* at cap, but from the user's side that block was near-silent — the creation form for a blocked record (character/lore/history/location/faction/RPG character/era/idea-conversion) closed as if it had succeeded, with only a 4-second toast as the only signal, and two paths (`Lore.jsx`, `WorldHistory.jsx`) actually crashed on the blocked `null` return (`entry.id`/`event.id` on null). Also found `saveRpgCharacter`, `addComicPanel`, `addComicPage`, and `addEra` had no `storageExceededCheck()` gate at all, so those record types could bypass the cap client-side with zero warning.
