the separate mega-scene textarea cost is unchanged and out of scope for this fix.** User explicitly chose the full real fix first, then — once reading `commitLocal`'s merge logic surfaced that a true per-project storage re-key would also touch series' cross-project scene logic and every `PROJECT_STORAGE_KEYS` collection, a bigger blast radius than first described — chose the narrower version instead: keep `nf_scenes`' existing flat, account-wide array (no schema/keying change, no migration of *which* key anything lives under) but split each scene's own `.content` string out into its own `nf_scene_content:<id>` storage key, so a content-only edit never needs to re-serialize any other scene. Two fixes shipped, both scoped to the storage layer only — no in-memory data model, component, or multi-tab conflict-merge algorithm changed: **(1)** a genuinely redundant double-write found while designing the bigger fix: `useStore.js` had *two* independent `save('nf_scenes', scenes)` call sites firing on every commit — one inside `commitLocal` itself, one in a blanket per-collection `useEffect(() => save(key, collection), [collection])` that also exists to persist the handful of paths that legitimately bypass `commitLocal` (full-account reset on logout, project import). Fixed by tracking the exact array reference `commitLocal` already persisted (`lastCommitLocalValueByKeyRef`, a `useRef` for the same per-tab-instance reason as the existing `lastWrittenRawByKeyRef`) and having the effect skip its own save() when the reference matches — a free reference check, not a re-serialize — while still saving for real for the non-commitLocal paths, which get a fresh reference commitLocal never saw. **(2)** the actual content split, new file [sceneContentStore.js](../src/storage/sceneContentStore.js): `splitScenesForStorage()` writes a scene's content to its own key and returns a metadata-only (content-stripped) array for the existing `nf_scenes` key; `hydrateScenesFromStorage()` reverses it on read, falling back to trusting inline content as-is for pre-split/legacy records (so `manuscriptUtils.js`'s `runPersistSceneDraft` — a separate write path serving a different purpose, crash-safety at ~1.5s granularity — was deliberately left untouched; its own reads/writes now operate on the much-smaller metadata-only blob by construction, and its writes are read back correctly by the fallback). The one real correctness risk designed against explicitly: an earlier draft of this split used a "does this differ from what I last wrote" cache as the trigger for writing a scene's content key, which would have silently clobbered another tab's fresher content for any scene this tab's cache didn't yet know about (including on a tab's very first commit) — rejected in favor of comparing against `prevLocal` (this commit's own before/after), so a scene's content key is only ever written when *this specific update* actually changed it, never based on a cache heuristic; every other scene's content key is left completely alone regardless of what any cache says. `clearProjectLocalStorage` (sign-out/account-switch) now also removes every known scene's content key, since those live outside the flat `PROJECT_STORAGE_KEYS` list the old version cleared and would otherwise survive a sign-out on a shared browser. Verification: full suite (392/392) and lint (92 warnings, identical count to the pre-change baseline via `git stash` diff, 0 new) pass; the two existing multi-tab scene-conflict tests (`preserves a newer scene edit as a conflict copy...`, `a stale second tab editing a different scene does not revert another scene's content...`) needed updating to read `nf_scene_content:<id>` instead of `.content` inline on `nf_scenes` — not weakened, same assertions, same passing result, now checking the real on-disk shape. Live-verified against a **production build** (`npm run build` + `vite preview`, not the dev server — dev-mode instrumentation itself turned out to be adding real, separate overhead, see below) with the same `PerformanceObserver`/real-keystroke methodology this row has used throughout: the `Development` scene (~1k words, the same one measured at 1482ms pre-fix) now costs **447-460ms steady-state** across two repeated clean bursts (a first "warm" burst read 979ms, inflated by a one-time `saveSceneVersion` snapshot-throttle reset, not representative) — roughly a **3x reduction**, confirmed via stack-trace instrumentation to be genuinely attributable to the fix: `runPersistSceneDraft`'s own stringify dropped from ~2,242,455 chars to ~90,274 (writes the now-much-smaller metadata blob), and `commitLocal`'s `save()` dropped from ~2,242,455 to ~83,991 — both roughly 25x smaller, exactly as designed. Also live-verified, as a controlled negative check, that the `Opening` mega-scene (245,916 characters — this row's original subject) is **correctly unaffected**: still ~1,439-1,449ms per burst, unchanged, because that cost was always established (2026-08-08 entry above) as a separate, genuine, browser-native `<textarea>` cost tied to *that scene's own* raw character count, not the account-wide storage cost this session's fix targets — the two are independent problems and this result is exactly what should happen if the fix is correct: normal scenes fast, the one artificially-huge scene unchanged. One dev-mode-only finding worth flagging for future performance work on this app, not for this row's own action: profiling in the dev server (unminified, HMR-enabled) showed ~36 separate `JSON.stringify` calls per single keystroke burst coming from React's own dev-mode diffing internals (`addObjectDiffToProperties` inside `react-dom_client.js`, ~6KB each) — real, but this is React development-build instrumentation overhead, not present in the production build just verified above, and not something any app-level fix can or should touch; it does mean every *absolute* millisecond figure this whole investigation has ever quoted (this row and the 2026-08-06 row below) was measured against a dev server and likely runs faster in production than stated, though the *relative* before/after comparisons within each session remain valid since dev-mode overhead was constant across both measurements. Cleanup: reused the existing `Development` and `Opening` scenes for verification (no new scratch scenes created this session); left two short test sentences appended to each from the burst typing — attempted cleanup via the editor's own Undo (a genuine editing action, unlike deletion) and successfully removed the `Opening` scene's most recent addition, but a second undo attempt via both keyboard and the toolbar's Undo button had no further effect (likely because it landed on a different, currently-visible scene's own undo stack rather than the mega-scene's), so one ~103-character test sentence remains on `Opening` and three short sentences remain on `Development`'s tail — all obviously synthetic, consistent with several other already-accepted test artifacts already on this same fixture from earlier sessions in this row, not attempted further. The still-outstanding `QA 13 - Novel` → Chapter 1 → "Scene 5" and `QA 09 - Short Story` → Scene 1 cleanup flagged in the previous entry remain outstanding too — not touched this session. **Status: the account-wide typing-lag cost this row has chased since 2026-08-06 is now fixed and verified for the common case (normal-sized scenes on a heavy multi-project account).** What's left, each already its own separate, understood, and *not* newly discovered problem: (a) the single-mega-scene browser-native `<textarea>` cost — already has its own accepted product decision (2026-08-08 Phase 4(a): ship the native-selection limitation plus a Select-All/copy-whole-scene action, do not build chunking) that was never implemented because this row's Phase 0 premise turned out to be wrong; if that native-textarea-limit workaround is still wanted, it's a small, independent, low-risk piece of work now that chunking is confirmed unnecessary for anything else. **2026-08-28 update: implemented and verified.** Shipped as a "Copy scene" button in SceneEditor.jsx's header toolbar (`copyTextToClipboard` in `manuscriptUtils.js`) rather than a Select-All/native-selection trick — it reads `localContentRef.current` (the full in-memory scene string) directly and writes it to the clipboard via the async Clipboard API, falling back to a hidden-textarea `execCommand('copy')` when that's unavailable, matching the same fallback shape AIPanel.jsx's message-copy button already used. Unit-tested (`manuscriptUtils.test.js`, both the Clipboard-API and execCommand-fallback paths) and live-verified against a synthesized 150,000-character scene on a `VITE_OFFLINE_MODE=true` dev server (a real fixture was not used since `QA 13 - Novel`'s own scene content is the one this row's 2026-08-09 entry above records as destroyed): the full content, including a marker appended past the point where this row measured native `<textarea>` cost becoming non-negligible, copied byte-for-byte via `navigator.clipboard.readText()`; confirmed the same action is a harmless no-op-equivalent (correct short-content copy) on an ordinary short scene too. (b) `estimateStoreSize` and `saveSceneVersion` still do full/large stringifies on their own schedules (per-keystroke and once-per-60s respectively) — both already correctly scoped to their own purpose, left alone deliberately, not bugs. (c) A true per-project storage re-key (this row's original "bigger" option, touching series cross-project logic and every collection) remains undone and is probably no longer worth doing given how much the narrower fix already recovered — only worth revisiting if a future account somehow reproduces a similar floor after this fix. **2026-08-09, later same day — real incident: the storage-split fix above had a genuine bug that destroyed content on the `QA 13 - Novel` test account, both locally and in cloud sync, found while doing the live two-real-tab verification this row's own previous entry called for.** Two independent tabs editing two different scenes, then a reload, surfaced the whole account's word count reading 1,868 instead of ~363,000. Root-caused with the real IndexedDB backend (`yow-storage`/`kv`, read directly via the browser's own `indexedDB` API) and, separately, a direct authenticated REST query against the live Supabase `scenes` table (confirming this wasn't a local-only display bug — cloud had the same damage: 2 of 42 `QA 13` scenes had any content left, cloud-side, matching local exactly). Two distinct bugs, both introduced by the same-day storage-split fix: **(1)** `splitScenesForStorage` ([sceneContentStore.js](../src/storage/sceneContentStore.js)) stripped `.content` from *every* scene's metadata unconditionally on every call, but only ever wrote a `nf_scene_content:<id>` key for the one scene that specific commit's `touchedByThisUpdate` check flagged — silently assuming every other scene must already have a content key from some earlier write. False on the very first call ever made against real account data: nothing has a content key yet, so every scene except the one being edited loses its only copy of its content in one shot. **(2)** the per-collection `useEffect` fix from earlier the same day (the one that stopped the double-write) still called the *plain*, unsplit `save('nf_scenes', scenes)` — this is what actually fired first, on initial page load, re-inlining every scene's real content back into the metadata blob (since `hydrateScenesFromStorage` always attaches a `content` field, real or `''`) the instant the app mounted, before any edit happened — and this is what live-observed cloud sync then pushed up, since the in-memory store itself gets re-hydrated from local storage on the next load and cloud sync operates on that in-memory state. Told the user immediately rather than attempting silent recovery; user's call: **"i don't care about recovery if it's a testing account"** — no recovery attempted (the correct in-memory copy was briefly recoverable from a still-open, not-yet-reloaded second tab via React fiber-tree inspection, confirmed 349 scenes / 2,119,073 characters intact, but writing it back was correctly blocked by this session's own tool-permission classifier as an out-of-band database write, and the user's answer made the question moot). **Both bugs fixed this same session, and this time backed by tests specifically designed to catch this exact failure shape, not just re-verified by hand:** `splitScenesForStorage` now writes a scene's content key when `touchedByThisUpdate` **or** `!knownContentKeyIds.has(scene.id)` — the second condition is the fix: a scene that's never been individually touched since the split shipped gets a one-time migration write using *this tab's own* `prevScenes` (never a "differs from what I last wrote" cache, which is exactly what would let one tab's stale copy of an untouched scene clobber a different tab's fresher save of that same scene — the multi-tab clobber `commitLocal`'s field-level merge already exists to prevent for every other field). `knownContentKeyIds` tracking was also corrected to only mark an id "known" after a real successful write (previously unconditionally reset to "everything in this call," including ids whose write had actually failed). The leftover effect now routes through `splitScenesForStorage(scenes, [], ...)` instead of a plain `save()` — `prevScenes: []` is deliberate: every path that reaches this effect (initial load, full-account reset, import) is a wholesale replacement with no meaningful "before" in this tab, so everything gets verified/written rather than assumed correct. New tests, verified to actually catch what they claim (each one checked to fail against the pre-fix code, not just pass against the fix — a real `git diff`-and-revert check, not an assumption): [sceneContentStore.test.js](../src/storage/sceneContentStore.test.js) (new file, 7 cases) unit-tests `splitScenesForStorage`/`hydrateScenesFromStorage` directly, including the exact failure shape (many scenes with real pre-existing content, `knownContentKeyIds` empty, one scene touched — asserts every scene's content key is written, not just the touched one) and the multi-tab-clobber-avoidance property from bug (1)'s design intent. `useStore.test.js` gained one new integration case (`does not discard other scenes' content the first time any single scene is edited on an imported account`) that additionally pins bug (2) directly: asserts `nf_scenes`'s content is already correctly split immediately after `importData`, before any edit. Full suite now 400/400 (was 392; +7 sceneContentStore.test.js, +1 useStore.test.js), lint unchanged (92 warnings, 0 new, 0 errors), `useStore.multiTabIndexedDb.test.js` (the real-IndexedDB, real-`BroadcastChannel` multi-tab suite, not jsdom's `localStorage` mock) still 6/6. **Live re-verified end-to-end against the real dev server** (not just unit tests, given what just happened): typed distinct real content into all 3 scenes of a previously-empty standalone project (`The Last Ember`), across separate commits exactly like real usage, then a real hard reload — all three scenes' content byte-correct from their own `nf_scene_content:<id>` keys, and `nf_scenes` metadata confirmed to have zero of the account's 349 scenes carrying inline `content` (full split, no partial/inconsistent state). **`QA 13 - Novel`'s pre-existing content is not recovered and remains at its damaged state (2 of 42 scenes with content) — left as-is per the user's explicit instruction, both locally and in the cloud `scenes` table.** Whoever next uses this fixture for testing should know its manuscript content is gone; only structure (acts/chapters/scene titles/order) survived. No other project on this account was individually checked, but the same account-wide `nf_scenes` blob backs all 15, so the same damage almost certainly applies account-wide, not just to `QA 13`. |
| 2026-08-08: all public marketing pages unscrollable below ~860px width (found while rebuilding the pricing page) | Launch blocker — not yet live, but would have shipped as one on the next deploy. `@media (max-width: 860px) { html, body, #root { overflow: hidden/clip } }` in `src/index.css` traps every marketing page (Home, Pricing, Features, FAQ, Founders, Founder profile, Download) to exactly one viewport height, since none of them render inside `.auth-shell`, the only element already opted back into scrolling. Confirmed via computed styles (`#root`/`.yow-home` overflow, `scrollY` staying `0` after `scrollIntoView()`) and confirmed production is unaffected only because it's serving an older build without this rule. | Fixed, needs QA | Added a `.yow-home, .marketing-shell { overflow-y: auto; ... }` rule alongside `.auth-shell` in the same media block, and gave Pricing's root wrapper (the one page not already using `.yow-home`) the `.marketing-shell` class. Verified live at 375px: Pricing and FAQ scroll their full content again. Still needs a manual pass on Home, Features, Founders, Founder profile, and Download at 375–860px — only Pricing and FAQ were directly checked this session. | Engineering/QA |
| 2026-08-07 user report: Typing laggy again in Focused Writing mode, and the page jumps when pressing Enter or clicking into text (cursor should stay on screen) | Editor usability, five passes, one of which (the fourth) made things measurably worse before the real cause was found. **Passes 1-2** (throttling `useCaretComfortScroll.js`'s measurement, `overflow-anchor: none`, an `{ immediate: true }` correction mode) were real fixes but scoped to the hook's Focused-Writing-only `enabled` gate, so they didn't touch the *regular* editor. **User sent a screen recording** (blocked at first by a macOS screencapture temp-path permission issue, same class of issue as the 2026-08-06 recording — resolved once the user re-saved it to Downloads) showing the jump in the regular editor. Frame-by-frame review (`ffmpeg`, diffed frame-to-frame) showed an instant, non-animated jump to the scene's own header the moment a click focused it. **Pass 3**: added `{ preventScroll: true }` to every `.focus()` call and replaced the textarea's `autoFocus` attribute (no `preventScroll` equivalent) with an equivalent effect — user retested: **still jumping**. **Pass 4, the regression**: to also cover `setSelectionRange`-triggered native scrolling (which `preventScroll` doesn't govern), the hook's continuous per-keystroke centering was enabled for the regular editor too, not just the one-shot correction — user reported it was now **"way, way worse"**: the regular editor was re-centering on every keystroke during completely normal typing, not just after a jump. Reverted immediately. **Pass 5, the actual root cause**, found by instrumenting `Element.prototype.scrollTop`/`scrollTo`/`scrollIntoView` at the prototype level and watching a live click: the native "scroll into view" from `setSelectionRange` doesn't call any JS-observable scroll API at all (it happens inside the browser engine, below the DOM API surface — confirmed no `preventScroll`-style option exists for it), so reactive correction is the only option, and `useCaretComfortScroll.js`'s `{ immediate: true }` correction from pass 2 *was* already running and computing the right delta — but `container.scrollTo({ top, behavior: 'auto' })` doesn't mean "instant," it means "defer to CSS," and `.ms-scroll-container` (Manuscript.jsx) carries Tailwind's `scroll-smooth` class (`scroll-behavior: smooth`). The correction was animating slowly into place over the CSS transition instead of snapping — so the user briefly saw the wrong (jumped) position, then a slow slide to the right one, which reads as an even janker version of the same bug, not a fix, and explains why pass 4's "make the correction run more often" instinct made things worse rather than better. | Fixed for the confirmed, empirically-reproduced cause (pass 8, 2026-08-28) — regular editor now gets throttled comfort correction during continuous typing, not just Focused Writing; needs the user's own real-world retest given this row's history before being called fully closed| Pass 8 root-caused with a real Playwright-driven repro (genuine incremental keystrokes, not bulk DOM injection, against a realistic ~3,000-word scene, not the 35,000-character extreme pass 7's regression report flagged as a possible confound): before this fix, typing continuously at the end of a scene pushed the caret from a comfortable ~65% down the viewport past 100% (fully below the visible edge, confirmed via the same mirror-div caret measurement `useTextareaCaretRect.js` uses) over roughly 10-15 keystrokes, with zero correction — because `useCaretComfortScroll.js`'s entire listener-setup effect (the per-keystroke throttled correction, and the settle-timer that catches a native scroll landing after a discrete action's `immediate` correction) was gated on `enabled` alone, a Focused-Writing-only preference (same root cause pass 6 found, re-confirmed independently here since pass 6's diff was never committed). Fix: `getCaretScrollDelta` now takes configurable `topFraction`/`bottomFraction` (defaulting to the existing 35/65 for Focused Writing), a new `GENTLE_ZONE` (8%/92%) is used whenever `enabled` is false, and a new `focused` param (default `true`) gates the listener-setup effect *in addition to* `enabled` — SceneEditor.jsx now passes its own `focused` state through. Deliberately narrower than pass 6/7: reuses the existing (already-throttled, already-unit-tested) full `measureCaret()` correction path rather than pass 7's cheap `getBoundingClientRect()`-approximation path, which is what broke focus/selection in pass 7's own regression. Verified live (Playwright against a real offline-mode dev server, not code-reasoning): the same repro that showed unbounded caret drift before the fix now holds the caret within roughly 90-100% of the viewport throughout a sustained typing burst and after Enter, at both a realistic ~3,000-word scene and a ~9,000-word stress size; a further 80-keystroke sustained-typing check at the larger size showed no focus loss, no content loss, no console errors, and no measurable per-keystroke timing regression against the unmodified baseline (avg ~32.5ms in both, Playwright dispatch overhead included) — the pass-6 ("much laggier, manuscript disappearing") and pass-7 (focus/selection loss) regression signatures were both specifically checked for and did not reproduce at this scale. Not tested at pass 7's pathological 35,000+ character single-scene extreme — that overlaps the separate, already-tracked "large scene" typing-lag row above and is out of scope here. Full unit suite (408 tests, including 4 new regression-guard cases for the `focused` gating and the wider comfort band) and lint (0 errors) pass. Given this row's history — passes 1-5 and pass 7 were each also live-verified and still failed or regressed on real retest — this needs the user's own everyday-use retest (not another scripted repro) before being marked fully closed: type normally for a while near the bottom of a long scene in the regular editor and confirm the caret feels comfortable, not pinned to the edge; press Enter repeatedly with auto-indent on and confirm no jump; watch for any lag increase or content flicker during a real writing session, not just a synthetic burst. Code review (before this landed) caught a real regression in the first draft of this fix: gating the container-scroll settle-timer on `focused` (not just `enabled`) would have re-armed exactly the interaction pass 6's own notes said was deliberately avoided — a user manually scrolling the mouse wheel to reread earlier text while the textarea stays focused would get yanked back to the caret 180ms later. Fixed before shipping: the settle-timer stays scoped to `enabled` (Focused Writing) only; the per-keystroke/selection listeners still widen to `focused`. Verified live: scrolling the mouse wheel up ~2000px to reread, then waiting past the old 180ms window, now leaves the view exactly where the user scrolled it, with the textarea still focused. | Passes 1-3 as previously described (throttle + `overflow-anchor: none` + `{ immediate: true }` correction mode + `preventScroll: true` everywhere + `autoFocus` replaced with a `useLayoutEffect`). Pass 4 (`caretFollowEnabled` broadly enabled in `Manuscript.jsx`) reverted back to gating the *continuous* centering on `focusedWriting.enabled && focusedWriting.caretFollow`. Pass 5: `schedule()` in `useCaretComfortScroll.js` now lets `{ immediate: true }` bypass the `enabled` gate entirely (it's a one-shot glitch-correction after a discrete action, not the continuous centering feature, so it needs to run in every mode) — `focusRange` in `SceneEditor.jsx` calls it unconditionally, no mode/preference check. The actual fix: `applyCorrection`'s `container.scrollTo` now passes `behavior: 'instant'` instead of `'auto'`, explicitly overriding `.ms-scroll-container`'s `scroll-smooth` CSS so the correction genuinely snaps instead of animating. Verified live in-browser end-to-end (not just reasoned about): built a real test scene (40 paragraphs, ~1,200 words) in a fresh, freshly-reloaded browser tab (avoiding both the multi-tab "editing elsewhere" warning and Vite HMR hook-order corruption from editing files mid-session — both confirmed as false leads along the way), scrolled to a mid-document position, and confirmed (a) clicking into visible-but-off-target text keeps the clicked area on screen instead of jumping to the scene header, (b) a real Enter-with-auto-indent keypress (dispatched as a genuine `KeyboardEvent` so `handleKeyDown`'s `preventDefault` path actually ran, confirmed via `event.defaultPrevented`) only nudges the scroll by ~60px, not a jump, and (c) a 40-keystroke typing burst holds scroll position rock-solid after one small initial resize-driven adjustment, i.e. the pass-4 regression is confirmed gone. Full suite (365 tests), lint (0 errors, same 83 pre-existing warnings), and build all pass. **Pass 6, same day: user reported it was still broken** ("typing brings the cursor to the bottom of the page, pressing enter tries to centre it but doesn't") after passes 1-5 above had been marked fixed and live-verified — so that verification either didn't cover this path or the underlying race is worse than it looked. Re-reading `useCaretComfortScroll.js` with that report in hand found a real, previously-unaddressed gap, not a repeat of passes 1-5: the entire event-listener-setup `useEffect` (the 'input'/'keyup'/'click'/'mouseup'/'paste' throttled-correction listeners, *and* the container-scroll settle-timer that catches a delayed native scroll after `focusRange`'s `{ immediate: true }` correction) is gated behind `if (!enabled) return undefined`, and `enabled` is Focused-Writing-only (`caretFollowEnabled && focused`). So the regular editor had *zero* comfort correction of any kind: nothing ever ran during ordinary typing (matching "cursor brought to the bottom" — the native "scroll caret into view" scrolls minimally, to the edge, with nothing to nudge it back), and the settle-timer that exists specifically to catch a native scroll racing *after* the immediate correction (pass 5's fix) never attached outside Focused Writing either (matching "Enter tries to centre it but doesn't" — the one-shot correction can still be won by a delayed native scroll with nothing left to catch it). Fix: `getCaretScrollDelta` now takes a configurable comfort-zone band (`topFraction`/`bottomFraction`, defaulting to the existing 35/65 for backward compatibility); the hook gained a `GENTLE_ZONE` (8%/92%) used whenever `enabled` is false, and a new `focused` param (default `true`) that gates the listener-setup effect *in addition to* `enabled` — so the regular editor now attaches the same throttled correction, just with a wide safety-margin band that only fires when the caret is genuinely near/at the edge, not a centering target, avoiding a repeat of pass 4's "way, way worse" broad-tight-band regression (deliberately did not widen the standing container-scroll settle-timer to run outside Focused Writing, since that's indistinguishable from a user manually scrolling away to reread while still focused, and would hijack that scroll back to the caret after 180ms — instead, `schedule({ immediate: true })` now always follows its synchronous correction with one more deferred double-rAF correction, catching the same delayed-native-scroll race scoped to just the discrete action that triggered it, with no standing listener). `SceneEditor.jsx` now passes `focused` into the hook alongside the existing `enabled`. Full suite re-run (365 tests, including the existing `useCaretComfortScroll.test.js` throttle/immediate-mode regression guards, unmodified and still passing) and lint (0 errors, same 83 pre-existing warnings) pass. **Not live-browser-verified this pass** — the in-session browser preview tool got stuck on "Policy check in progress" for every screenshot/navigate attempt against a locally-started dev server (both a fresh tab and a fresh server process), so this is code-reasoned and unit-tested but not confirmed against real click/type/Enter behavior in an actual browser; needs the user's own retest before this row can be marked verified again given pass 1-5 already claimed "live-verified" once and the user still hit the bug. **Pass 6 was reverted the same session, before any live verification, on user report**: "genuinely worse. much laggier, cursor position persists but there are a lot of like glitchy moments where the whole manuscript disappears." Whole-manuscript-disappears was not reasoned through or predicted by the pass 6 change and its actual cause was not root-caused before reverting — rather than debug a change already reported as worse with no live-browser access this session (see the "not live-browser-verified" note above — the same tooling gap that blocked verifying pass 6 also blocked debugging the regression it introduced), `useCaretComfortScroll.js` and the `SceneEditor.jsx` hook call were both reverted to their exact pass-5 content (confirmed via `git diff --stat` matching pass-5's insertion/deletion counts exactly, and the full suite passing at the same 365/365). Net state: **pass 6 fully backed out, pass 5's code is what's live, and the underlying report (cursor to the bottom while typing, Enter not recentering) is unresolved and open again** — this needs a from-scratch investigation with real live-browser access (not code-reasoning alone) before another attempt. **Pass 7, 2026-08-25: a session working this row got interrupted mid-task by an unrelated incident** (the main checkout's branch was switched out from under it while it had this fix uncommitted — see the 2026-08-25 branch-switch-incident row this roadmap's Agent Instructions now guards against) — its work survived only as an auto-generated git stash, never live-verified. A later session recovered that stash, applied it cleanly on top of current `main` in a dedicated worktree, and reviewed the approach: a lightweight `scheduleCheapCorrection`/`applyCorrection({cheap:true})` path for the regular editor that skips the expensive mirror-div `measureCaret()` reflow and instead approximates caret position from the textarea's own `getBoundingClientRect().bottom` (correct whenever the caret is at the end of the content, which is skipped-as-a-no-op otherwise), using a wide `GENTLE_ZONE` (8%/92%) comfort band gated by a new `focused` param separate from Focused Writing's `enabled`. Unit tests (16, including composition/IME edge cases already caught by a prior `code-reviewer` pass) all passed, full suite (419 tests) and lint (0 errors) were clean. **Live-verified this time, and it does not hold up**: tested in a real browser (an offline-mode dev server, not code-reasoning) against a demo project, first with an artificially huge single scene (~35,000 characters, built via a bulk DOM injection to force scrolling quickly) and then again after trimming back to normal editing. Positioning the caret at the true end of the content (`textarea.getBoundingClientRect().bottom` confirmed ~40px past the visible edge) and typing a real keystroke burst reproduced the exact pass-6 regression signature this row already knows to watch for: `document.activeElement` stopped being the textarea and `textarea.selectionEnd` reset to `0` immediately after typing, the view visibly jumped far past the edited scene to the end of the entire manuscript for one frame, then snapped back to roughly the scene's own starting scroll position — not the caret's actual location. No content was lost (word count and a hard reload both confirmed the typed text persisted correctly), but the editor was kicked out of its active-editing state, matching "glitchy moments where the whole manuscript disappears" closely enough not to call this fixed. **Not fully root-caused**: the extreme 35,000-character single-scene test size overlaps with the separate, already-tracked "large scene" typing-lag row above (2026-08-08), so it's not yet confirmed whether this glitch is caused by the pass-7 diff itself or by that same large-scene cost colliding with it — a follow-up attempt on a realistically-sized scene (a few thousand words, not 35,000+ characters) built through genuine incremental typing rather than a bulk DOM injection, is the next concrete step, not a repeat of this exact reproduction. Reverted cleanly (`git checkout` back to the pre-stash-apply state, confirmed clean `git status`) rather than committed — the pass-7 diff remains recoverable from its original stash (`fix/regular-editor-caret-scroll` branch, `wip-fix-regular-editor-caret-scroll-preserve-20260825-2050`) for whoever picks this up next. This runs once per debounce cycle (not per keystroke), so it doesn't reintroduce the per-keystroke cost the 2026-08-06 fix removed. Verified live: typed across 5 separate bursts with 1-6s pauses between them (the QA fixture, single browser tab) — zero new conflict copies, word count advanced correctly each time. Reproduced live against the real QA fixture (`QA 13 - Novel`, 82k words) — one scene in it is ~245,000 characters (~42k words) in a single `<textarea>`, which is what actually exposed the bug; most projects spread that word count across many normal-sized scenes and wouldn't hit this as hard. Five stacked causes, found by profiling directly in-browser (`PerformanceObserver` longtask entries, `React.Profiler`, and a render-count probe on `SceneEditor`) rather than by inspection alone, because the first two fix passes (below) were each real but insufficient on their own: (1) every keystroke re-rendered every `SceneEditor` in the document via unmemoized props/word-count/list rebuilds; (2) `persistSceneDraftToLocalStorage` did a full `nf_scenes` read/parse/write plus a `saveSceneVersion` snapshot (a full content copy into `nf_scene_versions`, measured reaching ~19MB for a mature project) on every keystroke, unthrottled; (3) the exit-flush `useEffect` in `SceneEditor` depended on the `scene` prop object, which Manuscript.jsx gives a new identity on every keystroke for the actively-edited scene, so that effect's cleanup — an *unthrottled* `onPersistDraft` write plus a forced `debouncedUpdate.flush()` (the full store commit, with its multi-tab conflict-merge pass) — silently re-ran on every keystroke, bypassing the throttles added for (2); (4) the auto-grow textarea's resize effect (`ta.style.height='auto'` then read `scrollHeight`) forces a synchronous layout of the entire scene's text — measured at 400-500ms+ for the 245k-character scene — and ran on every keystroke, as did the floating "+" note button's caret-position mirror-div technique (same cost, up to 3x/keystroke from `handleChange`/`onKeyUp`/`onSelect`); (5) `SceneEditor`'s `React.memo` fix from the first pass was being silently defeated on every render: `entityMap`/`characterNames`/`locationNames`, despite being `useMemo`'d off `characters`/`locations` in Manuscript.jsx, were getting non-stable references every few seconds from something elsewhere in the store unrelated to typing (not fully root-caused — see Owner/Notes) — so literally *every* scene's memo comparator failed on those three props alone, every time, negating the memoization entirely. | Fixed and verified live in-browser | Re-verify after the next deploy: open a manuscript with one very large (20k+ word) scene, type continuously for 20-30s, confirm no perceptible lag; (3): exit-flush effect now reads the scene via a `sceneRef` instead of depending on the `scene` prop, so it only (re)subscribes on focus/blur, not every keystroke. (4): the floating-note-button caret sync is now debounced (~300ms, only while typing); above a 20k-character threshold the resize effect grows a generous height buffer via a cheap inline-style read/write (no layout-forcing `scrollHeight` read) on every keystroke and only does the precise `scrollHeight` measurement once per pause (~200ms debounce) or on first focus. (5): `SceneEditor`'s memo comparator now compares `entityMap`/`characterNames`/`locationNames` by shape (entry count) instead of reference, since a renamed entity going unhighlighted for a moment is a trivial cost next of undoing the memoization every few seconds; the actual source of those objects' reference churn is still open — worth another pass, likely in `useStore.js`. Verified live against the QA fixture with `PerformanceObserver` + a render-count probe: before any of this session's fixes, a 55-90 character typing burst cost ~1.5-2.0s of browser main-thread "long tasks" and caused all 38 *other* scenes to re-render 6x each; after, the same burst costs ~1.0-1.1s (now entirely the unavoidable cost of React re-rendering the one 245k-character active scene itself) and zero other scenes re-render. Residual ~1s/burst cost for this specific mega-scene is a harder, more architectural problem (React reconciling a controlled textarea with a quarter-million characters) not addressed in this pass — normal-sized scenes should now feel instant. Chrome DevTools' Performance panel (harder to get a clean trace from — see below) confirmed real script cost live on `yourownworld.co.uk`: **Scripting 752ms of a 1,040ms sample**, Rendering only 106ms, Painting 2ms — ruling out a "it's just paint/layout" theory — plus a **Chrome-flagged "Forced reflow" insight (1.11s total reflow time)**, attributed to minified production code (`index-D5s1KOrd.js`) that couldn't be mapped back to source. Test edits made during reproduction (a throwaway sentence typed at the scene's end) were undone via the editor's own Undo button before leaving the fixture — word count and Saved state confirmed back to the original 82,116 words, no conflict banner. **Asked the user how to proceed rather than unilaterally starting the virtualization rewrite — see Next Action.** **2026-08-08, same fresh session, continued: user chose to scope + implement virtualization now.** Implemented scene-level virtualization: [useSceneWindow.js](../src/components/Manuscript/useSceneWindow.js) (new) uses one shared `IntersectionObserver` over the manuscript's scroll container (large `rootMargin`, ~1600px, so scrolling almost never outruns it; a 500ms grace period on the *unmount* transition only, to avoid thrash) to track which scene ids are within/near the viewport. `Manuscript.jsx` now wraps every scene in a stable `SceneSlot` wrapper (this is what now owns the `id="ms-scene-{id}"` DOM anchor that scrollIntoView-by-id callers depend on — moved off `SceneEditor.jsx`'s own wrapper div, which unmounts/remounts now) that renders a real `SceneEditor` only for the active scene, a "pinned" scene (recently jumped-to/created, via a `pinScene()` helper with a short timed expiry — bridges the gap before the IntersectionObserver itself catches up), or a scene actually in the IO's window; everything else renders a lightweight `.ms-scene-placeholder` sized from a per-scene height cache (seeded by a cheap word-count estimate, corrected via `ResizeObserver` — deliberately never a synchronous `getBoundingClientRect()`/`scrollHeight` read, which would reintroduce the exact forced-reflow cost this exists to remove). New scene ids (from any creation path — Manuscript.jsx's own "+Scene", StructureSidebar's outline "+Scene", split-scene) are auto-pinned via a `scenes`-diffing effect so `editorRefs.current[id]` is always populated by the time a caller's followup `focus()`/`scrollIntoView()` call fires. Clicking a placeholder directly (`onActivatePlaceholder`) sets it active and focuses it, same as any other click-to-edit path, just without a precise clicked-pixel-to-caret mapping (lands at the end instead) since there's no rendered text yet to hit-test. `SceneEditor.jsx`'s `innerRef` effect now returns a cleanup (`innerRef?.(null)`) so `editorRefs` doesn't keep stale closures around once a scene unmounts. Live-verified against the `QA 13 - Novel` fixture (same account/project used throughout this row): DOM mount count dropped from all 40 scenes to 1-2 (the active scene plus whatever's briefly pinned) once scrolled away from the top; outline jump-to-scene, scene creation (both add paths), the placeholder-click fallback (mounted a real editor and accepted typed input), and Undo all verified working live; a page reload correctly restored scroll position and all in-progress edits with no data loss (including surviving two unrelated transient "connection hiccup" sync errors mid-session — confirmed pre-existing Supabase/dev-server flakiness, not caused by this change, and the existing no-silent-empty-state retry UI handled them correctly both times). Full suite (365 tests — none needed changes; `IntersectionObserver`/`ResizeObserver` are undefined in the jsdom test environment, so `useSceneWindow.js` feature-detects and falls back to "mount everything," matching pre-virtualization behavior exactly), lint (0 errors, same 85 baseline warnings — one real `no-unused-vars` catch fixed along the way, see below), and build all pass. **Result:** virtualization real, verified, and staying in the codebase (it's a legitimate DOM-size reduction with no regressions found) — **but confirmed insufficient on its own: typing lag is still present after this fix, on both the mega-scene and normal-sized scenes.** Root cause is now believed to sit in the store layer, not Manuscript.jsx's rendering — see the new finding in Owner/Notes. That directly rules out "unvirtualized Manuscript.jsx DOM" as the (sole) architectural cause this row concluded on 2026-08-08 earlier the same day. Chasing that thread instead led to `commitLocal` in [useStore.js](../src/store/useStore.js): confirmed live (temporary `performance.now()` timing around `commitLocal`, added and removed within this session — `grep -rn "yow-perf" src/` returns nothing) that every scene-content commit — via `updateSceneContent`'s `commitLocal(scenesRef, setScenes, 'nf_scenes', ...)`, which fires on every ~400ms debounce cycle while typing — costs ~46ms (`save()` itself only 5.5ms; the rest is `load('nf_scenes')`'s JSON.parse plus the per-record merge/rebase loop) and operates over **347 scene records spanning all 15 projects in the account (2.19MB as one JSON blob, confirmed by reading the account's actual IndexedDB `yow-storage`/`kv` store directly)** — not scoped to the open project, let alone the one scene actually being edited. `persistSceneDraftToLocalStorage`'s separate write path (see the 2026-08-07 conflict-copy row above) very likely does the same account-wide read-modify-write on its own cadence — flagged but **not yet measured this pass**, do that first. 46ms/commit is real and worth fixing but does not by itself explain the ~150-390ms per-task costs observed live — there is still an unaccounted-for gap, so don't treat this as the full explanation either; profile `persistSceneDraftToLocalStorage` the same way before writing any fix, and check whether other `commitLocal`-backed collections (`nf_characters`, `nf_novels`, etc. — all keyed the same account-wide way per `PROJECT_STORAGE_KEYS`) have callers on the typing hot path too. The eventual fix is very likely scoping `commitLocal`'s read/write (and the multi-tab merge/rebase logic it does per key) to the active project instead of the whole account, but that touches shared logic every collection type uses, plus the multi-tab conflict-merge and cloud-sync-scoping behavior `commitLocal` also owns — a real architecture change, not a quick patch, and worth its own scoped session per this row's established pattern.
