Changed: `Manuscript.jsx` (SceneSlot component, pin/auto-pin logic, `estimateSceneHeight`, wiring), `SceneEditor.jsx` (dropped its own `id`, added `innerRef` cleanup), `index.css` (`.ms-scene-placeholder` styling). Fixed one real lint error surfaced along the way: `virtualizationSupported` was destructured but never read in the `mount` boolean — without it, any browser lacking `IntersectionObserver` (and every Vitest/jsdom test that renders `<Manuscript>`) would have virtualized almost everything down to placeholders instead of falling back to "mount everything," since `useSceneWindow.js`'s `inView` set only ever gets populated by the observer callback. Caught by `no-unused-vars`, not by the tests (the one test suite that renders a full `<Manuscript>`, `ManuscriptCampaignWorkflow.test.jsx`, uses a single-scene fixture, so the bug wouldn't have shown up in its output even unfixed) — worth remembering this gap in test coverage if virtualization logic changes again. Left two harmless QA-fixture artifacts on `QA 13 - Novel` from live verification: a short "Store-commit-cost check..." sentence appended to Chapter 1's Development scene, and an empty "Scene 4" scene under Chapter 1 (one delete-button click didn't take and wasn't worth a second attempt) — both disposable QA fixture content, not real user work, consistent with this row's established practice of not fussing over that. **2026-08-08, same fresh session, continued again: user chose to finish diagnosing then implement the storage-layer fix now, in this same session.** Measured `persistSceneDraftToLocalStorage`'s cost the same way (temporary `performance.now()` timing, added and removed within this session): ~14ms per call on this account's 348-record `nf_scenes`, separate from and in addition to `commitLocal`'s ~46ms — confirmed live that on the debounced commit's trailing edge these run back-to-back (the draft flush, then the store commit), so a typing burst was paying for **two** full account-wide `nf_scenes` read-parse-write round trips per ~400ms cycle, not one. Implemented the fix: `commitLocal` (useStore.js) now caches the exact raw JSON string it last wrote per storage key (`lastWrittenRawByKeyRef`, a `useRef` — deliberately *not* module-level state, since `useStore.test.js`'s multi-tab tests simulate two tabs as two `renderHook()` instances sharing this module, and a shared cache would have conflated "tab A wrote this" with "tab B wrote this" and broken exactly the conflict-safety those tests exist to check — confirmed this mattered by running the full multi-tab suite after the change, all still pass). Before paying for the expensive `load(key, [])` JSON.parse + per-record merge/rebase loop, `commitLocal` now checks `readItem(key) === <what I last wrote>` — a cheap raw-string comparison — and skips straight to `next = rawNext` when they match, which is *provably* equivalent (not an approximation) to what the merge loop would have produced anyway when nothing external changed. `save()` now returns the exact raw string it wrote so the cache stays exact (handles the existing `nf_novels` quota-exceeded fallback path correctly too, since that writes a different payload than a naive `JSON.stringify(next)` would). Separately, since `persistSceneDraftToLocalStorage`'s write to the same `nf_scenes` key (immediately before `commitLocal`'s own write, every debounce cycle — see the 2026-08-07 conflict-copy row) would otherwise always register as "external" to `commitLocal` and defeat the skip, `manuscriptUtils.js`'s `persistSceneDraftToLocalStorage`/`runPersistSceneDraft` now return the raw string they wrote too, and `Manuscript.jsx` feeds that into a new `recordLocalWrite(key, raw)` function the store exposes — so commitLocal correctly recognizes its own tab's immediately-preceding sibling write instead of re-parsing/re-merging for it. (`runPersistSceneDraft`'s own ~14ms read-parse-write itself is *not* eliminated by this pass — it still always re-reads on every call, since making it skip too would need the reverse direction of this same wiring; flagged as a smaller remaining follow-up, not attempted here given the bigger win was `commitLocal`'s ~46ms.) Added one new regression test (`useStore.test.js`, "a cached tab still picks up another tab's edit that lands between two of its own commits") specifically covering the risk this change introduces: a tab that has already written (and cached) a key must still notice a genuinely external write landing between two of its *own* later commits, not just on its first-ever write (which the existing multi-tab tests already covered) — this is the scenario a naive implementation of this optimization would most plausibly get wrong. Full suite (366 tests, 365 pre-existing + the 1 new one — all pre-existing multi-tab/conflict tests pass unchanged), lint (0 errors, same 85 baseline warnings), and build all pass. **Live-verified the actual win** against `QA 13 - Novel` with real console timing (temporary, removed after): `commitLocal(nf_scenes)` dropped from ~40.7ms (first call each page load — no cache yet, correctly takes the full safe path) to a **consistent ~16.5-16.8ms on every subsequent commit** across two separate typing bursts — roughly a 59% reduction, reproducible, not a one-off. **Status:** Both storage-layer fixes (this row's earlier `estimateStoreSize` caching fix, and this pass's `commitLocal`/`persistSceneDraftToLocalStorage` skip-optimization) are real, verified, and now in the codebase — but **typing lag on this heavy account is very likely still not fully resolved**: ~16.8ms/commit plus `runPersistSceneDraft`'s still-unoptimized ~14ms is ~31ms/cycle, well under the ~150-390ms per-longtask costs measured earlier in this row, so a substantial, still-unidentified cost remains somewhere. Scene virtualization (verified real, still in the codebase) and now two rounds of storage-layer optimization (verified real) have each measurably helped without fully explaining the reported severity — the pattern this whole row has followed since 2026-08-06. Not re-verified against the original full-burst longtask measurement this pass (ran out of session time/turns) — that's the immediate next step, not a new investigation thread. **Next step:** **Re-run the same longtask-burst measurement this row has used throughout (PerformanceObserver, `QA 13 - Novel`, a small ~1.1k-word scene, not the mega-scene) against current `main` first — with both storage fixes and virtualization now in place, confirm how much (if any) of the ~1000-1400ms per burst remains, before doing anything else.** If a large gap still remains (likely, given the arithmetic above), the next concrete step is finally the source-mapped Chrome Performance trace this row has wanted since 2026-08-08 morning and never gotten cleanly — try it on `127.0.0.1` (unminified) now that the known synchronous-cost contributors (resize/caret-measurement debouncing, `estimateStoreSize` caching, unvirtualized DOM, redundant storage round-trips) are exhausted, which should make a trace far more legible than earlier attempts (those crashed processing a trace dominated by noise from causes that are now fixed). If profiling remains unworkable, `runPersistSceneDraft`'s still-open ~14ms (see Owner/Notes) is the next cheapest confirmed lead, followed by auditing whether any *other* code path reads/writes `nf_scenes` or the other large `PROJECT_STORAGE_KEYS` collections (`nf_characters`, `nf_novels`) on the typing hot path outside `commitLocal`/`persistSceneDraftToLocalStorage`. Same test account throughout (`QA 13 - Novel`, `mbishoptesting@gmail.com`, heavy multi-project account) — small accounts won't reproduce any of this. **Files touched:** Changed this pass: `useStore.js` (`save()` returns the raw string written; `commitLocal` gains the per-key skip-check and `lastWrittenRawByKeyRef`; new `recordLocalWrite` exposed from the store), `manuscriptUtils.js` (`runPersistSceneDraft`/`persistSceneDraftToLocalStorage` return the raw string written), `Manuscript.jsx` (new `handlePersistDraft` wrapper wired to `recordLocalWrite`, replaces the direct `onPersistDraft={persistSceneDraftToLocalStorage}` prop), `useStore.test.js` (one new regression test, described above). One real bug caught and fixed while wiring this up (not by the tests — by a manual re-read of every `save(...)` call site after changing its signature): `useEffect(() => save('nf_series', series), [series])` used an implicit-return arrow, so `save`'s new return value (a string) became that effect's *cleanup function* — React tried to call a string as a function on unmount, crashing 23 tests across two files with `TypeError: destroy is not a function`. Fixed by giving it a block body like every other effect in that list already has. Left the same two harmless QA-fixture artifacts from the earlier pass (untouched this time — the live browser session hit intermittent "connection hiccup" sync errors again near the end, unrelated to this change per the same evidence as before, and cleanup wasn't worth fighting the automation further this pass). |
| 2026-08-05: AI manuscript tools needed explicit context-limit choices | AI quality / expectation-setting. Plot Hole, Lore Conflict, and Style Analysis were useful as excerpt-based checks, but users had no way to choose the context strategy or understand the tradeoff between broad structural scan and close reading. This made large-project findings too easy to over-trust and small-project warnings confusing. | Fixed in code; needs browser QA | Browser-QA Plot Hole, Lore Conflict, and Style Analysis on a small project and a multi-act project: switch Project scan / Focused chapter / Act review, confirm target dropdowns use the correct chapter/act labels, run at least one mode, and confirm the warning copy matches included/skipped text. | Added shared AI context modes: Project scan (broad full-structure map with short excerpts), Focused chapter/issue (longer excerpts for one chapter/issue), and Act/volume review (mid-detail pass for one act/volume). `aiToolPrompts.js` now owns the same limits used by the UI notice, and prompt text records the selected mode/target so model output is framed correctly. `AiContextSelector` is wired into Plot Hole, Lore Conflict, and Style. Focused unit tests pass. |
| 2026-08-05: AI tool coverage warning called small projects "large" | UX confusion. Plot Hole, Lore Conflict, and Style Analysis intentionally truncate individual scene excerpts above the prompt cap, but the shared notice always began "Manuscript is large" even when all scenes were included and the project had only a small word count. | Fixed | No further action needed. | `ManuscriptCoverageNotice` now distinguishes true omitted-scene large-project warnings from per-scene excerpt limits: omitted scenes still say "Manuscript is large," while all-scenes-but-truncated cases say "AI context is limited." Regression coverage added in `ManuscriptCoverageNotice.test.jsx`; focused tests pass. |
| 2026-08-04: Uploaded user-media images were reachable by anyone with the public Storage URL | Privacy/trust issue. Direct image URLs loaded in private/incognito browsers because the `user-media` bucket was public-read and records stored permanent public object URLs. Not direct data loss, but not acceptable for private writing/worldbuilding images. | Fixed in code; migration and live QA pending | Apply `supabase/migrations/20260804_private_user_media.sql`, then QA: old public object URLs should fail in private browser; signed-in app should still render legacy uploaded images and newly uploaded images; replacing/deleting images should still remove objects and decrement storage usage; PDF export should still include private images. | Product decision 2026-08-04: user uploads must be private. New uploads now save stable `yow-media:{path}` references, render/export paths resolve owner-only signed URLs, deletion handles both new references and legacy public URLs, and the migration flips `user-media` to private with owner-scoped SELECT for signed URL creation. **2026-08-05 user report and fix**: user reported photo upload broken on every upload surface they tried. Reproduced live in local dev against the real project (this migration is code-complete but not yet deployed — bucket is still public in production): a freshly uploaded object's `path` matched exactly between `upload()` and the immediate `createSignedUrl()` call (ruling out a path-derivation bug), `upload()` reported success with a real object id, and `download()`/`getPublicUrl()` both reliably reached the object indefinitely afterward — but `createSignedUrl()` and `list()` returned a hard S3 `NoSuchKey`/"Object not found" for that same path, persisting 20s+ across 5+ retries (ruled out as a propagation race). Root cause appears to be a genuine inconsistency between Supabase Storage's download/public-URL path and its sign/list path for this project — not an application bug, and not something a client-side retry fixes. Fix (stopgap, in `getSignedUserMediaUrl`): on a signing failure, fall back to the object's plain public URL instead of showing a blank image, since the object is provably reachable that way. This is safe going forward — once the bucket is actually made private (this row's pending migration), the public-URL fallback will 403 the same way signing currently fails, so it degrades no worse than today and stops being relevant once Supabase's sign/list behavior is understood or the object is confirmed reachable a different way. **This needs following up with Supabase directly before trusting signed URLs as the sole read path for the private-media migration above** — the fallback masks the symptom but not the underlying platform inconsistency. Covered by a new `uploadUserMedia.test.js` case forcing `createSignedUrl` to error and asserting the public-URL fallback. Full suite (346 tests) and lint pass. |
| 2026-08-04 user report: App repeatedly bounces to the "connection hiccup" screen ("We couldn't load your projects... Try again"), reported while testing the Import ZIP flow but not actually import-specific | Not data loss (the load-failure screen exists specifically so a partial/failed cloud read never gets shown as a silently-empty project — see the 2026-07-19 row below), but a false-positive trip of that screen makes the whole app unusable until "Try again" is clicked, on every page including import, and looked to the user like the import page itself was disconnecting. | Closed — QA-verified 2026-08-28 | No further action needed from this pass. Still worth a real-account browser-QA pass on the deployed build per the original note (throttle/flake the network in devtools during a page load with many projects) before final launch, since this pass could only exercise the fix at the source/unit level — see the verification note for exactly what was and wasn't covered. | Root cause: `loadUserData()` (`src/utils/firestoreSync.js`) fires ~21 fully-parallel Supabase queries (`user_settings` + 20 per-table reads) in one `Promise.all` on every page load/refresh — and by design (see the 2026-07-19 row's "never silently render empty data" fix), if even one of those 21 requests fails, the whole load throws and the user is bounced to the connection-hiccup retry screen, even though the other ~20 succeeded. With that many requests in flight on a real account, the odds any single one hits a one-off transient blip are far higher than for a lone request, so real accounts with more content saw this far more than a quick test account would. Fix: added `withRetry()`, wrapping each of the 21 queries with up to 3 attempts and a short backoff before a failure counts as real — absorbs one-off blips without weakening the existing guarantee that a genuine, persistent failure still throws and still shows the retry screen instead of hydrating a zeroed-out category. Covered by two new `firestoreSync.test.js` cases: one table erroring once then succeeding (load must complete normally), and one table erroring on every attempt (load must still throw, naming the failed table). Full file's test suite (5/5) and lint pass. **2026-08-28 QA verification:** credential-free environment (no live Supabase project), so `loadUserData()` never fires real network calls in `VITE_OFFLINE_MODE=true` — the connection-hiccup screen's trigger path can't be reached through a live authenticated browser session here. Instead reproduced and verified at the source: confirmed `firestoreSync.test.js`'s two dedicated cases (one-off blip on a single table recovers; persistent failure on every retry attempt still throws, naming the table) pass against the current code (10/10 tests), then temporarily reverted `withRetry`'s default `attempts` from 3 to 1 and reran — the "recovers from a one-off transient error" case failed exactly as the original bug describes (`[sync] failed to load: characters` thrown from a single blip), confirming the test suite actually detects the regression rather than passing vacuously; restored `attempts = 3` and reconfirmed all 10 pass. `npm run lint` (0 errors) and the full `vitest run` suite (409 tests) also pass. Live browser/devtools network-throttling verification against a real account remains outstanding and needs real Supabase credentials — see Next Action. |
| 2026-08-02: Two browser tabs on the same account silently clobber each other's edits to characters/locations/factions/lore/timeline/scenes/etc. | Data loss. Confirmed by QA (`data-safety-qa-checklist.html` items `structured-record-conflict` and `conflict-same-scene`) and root-caused live in real two-tab browser sessions after three earlier fix attempts all still reproduced on retest. Four layered causes, found in order: (1) cloud layer — `useStore.js`'s per-collection sync effects re-pushed a table's *entire* in-memory array on every change, so a stale second tab's next debounce reverted every other record in the collection in the cloud; (2) local-array layer — independent of cloud sync, `commitLocal` (the shared updater behind nearly every entity CRUD function, including scenes') always wrote its own possibly-stale full array straight over the shared storage blob; (3) **the storage-adapter gap, found after (1) and (2) still didn't fix it live** — every regular (non-desktop) browser session is backed by `browserVaultAdapter.js`'s IndexedDB adapter (added earlier for a browser-storage-quota fix), which hydrates a per-tab in-memory mirror once at startup and never re-reads IndexedDB afterward, with no cross-tab notification of any kind (unlike `localStorage`'s native `storage` event) — so fixes (1) and (2), which both depend on reading a fresher on-disk value, were structurally unable to see another tab's edit no matter how long the user waited; (4) **whole-record vs. field-level, found after (3) was fixed and the user clarified they were testing edits to the *same* character** — even with cross-tab reads working, `commitLocal`'s original conflict handling for a record *this update touched* was still whole-record last-write-wins (with a review-banner escape hatch): a form that only changes one field still submits every other field exactly as loaded, so saving one field could silently revert a different field another tab had just saved for the *same* record, with no warning unless the user noticed and opened the sync-conflict banner. Each layer's live symptom looked identical ("edit disappears"), which is why it took five passes to fully separate them. This also explains why unit tests kept passing while the live bug persisted for (3): jsdom's plain `window.localStorage` (used by the test suite) doesn't go through the IndexedDB adapter at all, so those tests never exercised the actual code path in play. **(5) The `importData` reconciliation gap, found after (1)-(4) were fixed and the user reported lore/characters were now fine but the manuscript specifically still lost data, and narrowed it to "one tab shows the edit, the other doesn't, after a refresh"**: (1)-(4) all fixed the *runtime edit* path (`commitLocal`, used while the app is already running), but a browser refresh or login goes through a completely different function, `importData`, which is called once with either a fresh cloud fetch or a local snapshot (`shouldPreferLocal`) and does a flat `setScenes(sourceData.scenes)` — a full replace with no merge logic at all, unlike `commitLocal`. A scene conflict copy's push to the cloud (`saveSceneDoc`, called directly, no debounce, no retry) is exactly the kind of write that can fail silently on a transient network/auth error (`.catch(console.error)` — and real errors of this shape, `AbortError`, auth-token-refresh races, were directly observed live in this session's own testing). If that push hasn't landed by the time of a refresh and the refresh's cloud fetch (or even a stale local read) doesn't include the copy yet, the flat replace silently drops it — explaining "gone after refresh" and "the two tabs disagree" (whichever tab's refresh raced the slower push loses the copy, the other doesn't). Confirmed live: seeding a scene conflict copy directly in IndexedDB and reloading made it vanish with the pre-fix code, and survive (with a working "⚠ 1 conflict copy" banner and restore/discard modal) after the fix. **(6) The "Saved" indicator lied about when it was actually safe to refresh, found after the user reported the same silent overwrite a sixth time on the same local dev server (ruling out a deployment mismatch) and confirmed they were doing exactly what (5)'s QA note recommends — waiting for "Saved" before refreshing.** All of (1)-(5) fix what happens once data reaches storage or reconciliation; none of them touch *when the user is told it's safe to refresh*. `Manuscript.jsx`'s scene-editor "Saved" label (`saveState`) was never wired to the store's real `syncStatus` (which `trackSync` only flips to `'synced'` once the actual Supabase write resolves) — it was a flat `setTimeout(() => setSaveState('saved'), 2000)` started the moment an edit fired, regardless of whether the 400ms local debounce, the *separate* 1000ms cloud debounce (`debouncedSaveScene`), and the network round-trip had actually finished. Reproduced live: editing a scene in one tab, waiting ~1-2s (past the fake "Saved"), then reloading a second tab showed the edit had reverted to stale content — and, worse, that reload's `importData` then re-persisted the stale choice back to the real on-disk IndexedDB via the unconditional `useEffect(() => save('nf_scenes', scenes), [scenes])` (`useStore.js`, one of ~20 identical per-collection effects), overwriting the correct edit with no merge and no `importing`-guard. The same edit-then-reload sequence with a real 8s settle window (long enough for the actual cloud push to land) did not reproduce, confirming the fake indicator's premature "Saved" — not the merge logic from (1)-(5), which held up correctly throughout — was the last open gap. After this, the user asked to stop chasing reconciliation and add a proactive warning instead — see (7) in the Fix column. | Warning-modal mitigation added and live-verified 2026-08-03 (scenes only; see (7)) — reconciliation fixes (1)-(6) remain in place underneath it | Browser-QA: re-run both `data-safety-qa-checklist.html` repros (`structured-record-conflict`: two tabs, edit different fields on the same record and on different records; `conflict-same-scene`: two tabs, same scene, including a real refresh and a real logout/login) on the deployed build, plus a general pass confirming no other multi-tab edit combination loses data. Also verify the "⚠ N sync conflicts" banner (top utility bar, for structured records) and the manuscript toolbar's "⚠ N conflict copies" banner (for scenes) both still appear and their Restore/Keep-mine actions work, including surviving a page refresh. Additionally: confirm the manuscript "Saving…"/"Saved" indicator stays on "Saving…" through a real cloud round-trip (throttle network in devtools to see it) and only flips to "Saved" once sync genuinely completes — not after a flat ~2s. Additionally (7): two tabs, same scene — focus it for editing in tab one, then focus it in tab two; the "Also open in another tab" warning should appear in tab two immediately. "Go back" should return to read-only preview; "Edit anyway" should leave the textarea genuinely focused and typeable (not silently dropped back to preview), and should not re-nag on further keystrokes in that sitting. Refocusing later (new sitting) should warn again. Opening a *different* scene, or the same scene when nobody else has it open, should show nothing. | User reported the first four fix attempts (cloud diffing, `commitLocal` local-array rebase, the IndexedDB BroadcastChannel bridge, then the field-level merge) all still reproduced on retest at least once each, each time pushing to find the real cause rather than declaring victory on passing tests alone — this was the pattern that eventually surfaced (5). Landed, in order: (a) `debouncedSaveItems` diffs each table's array against the last snapshot actually pushed, only upserting changed records; (b) `commitLocal` rebases against the freshest on-disk snapshot for every record an update didn't touch; (c) a `BroadcastChannel('yow-storage-sync')` bridge in `browserVaultAdapter.js` so `setItem`/`removeItem` broadcast to other tabs, applied via new `applyExternalWrite`/`applyExternalRemove` methods on the IndexedDB backend (`src/storage/indexedDbBackend.js`); (d) `commitLocal`'s handling of a record this update *did* touch is now a true field-by-field merge — only a genuine same-field clash is flagged as a `recordConflicts` entry; (e) `importData`'s `setScenes(...)` now unions in any local scene conflict-copy record missing from whatever source data (local or cloud) was chosen, instead of a flat replace — the copy is never silently dropped just because the reconciliation step doesn't know about it yet. Also fixed a related race: `commitLocal` now short-circuits true no-op updates (every element reference-unchanged) without touching storage, since `saveCharacter` and similar functions fire a second, usually-no-op pass for bidirectional relationship sync that was otherwise re-reading/re-broadcasting for nothing. Live-verified end-to-end with two real browser tabs against the actual dev build and the real `yow-storage` IndexedDB database (not a mock) at every stage, including (for this last fix) seeding a conflict copy directly in IndexedDB, reloading, and confirming it now survives with a working review modal — proven to actually depend on the fix via a revert-and-rerun sanity check (test fails without it, passes with it). Covered by new/updated tests: `indexedDbBackend.test.js`, `browserVaultAdapter.test.js` (with a polling wait instead of a fixed timeout, after the fixed-timeout version flaked once under full-suite load), `useStore.multiTabIndexedDb.test.js` (7 cases: unrelated-record non-clobber, same-scene conflict, refresh survival, stale-cloud-on-refresh, different-scenes, same-scene-different-fields — all against the real IndexedDB adapter via `fake-indexeddb`), and a new `useStore.test.js` case reproducing the exact `importData`-drops-conflict-copy failure. Full suite (`vitest run`, 324 tests) and lint (0 errors, 78 warnings, same baseline) pass. **(6)**: user reported the same symptom a sixth time; ruled out a deployment mismatch first (confirmed same local dev server) before writing any new code, then read `SceneEditor.jsx`/`Manuscript.jsx` in full since real keystroke-driven typing had never actually been exercised live in this investigation (only unit tests and direct IndexedDB seeding) — found `Manuscript.jsx`'s `saveState` was a `setTimeout`-driven fake, wired to nothing. Fix: `Manuscript.jsx` now derives `saveState` from `store.syncStatus` (an effect sets `'saving'` while `syncStatus.state === 'syncing'`, `'saved'` otherwise), removing the fake timer entirely; the immediate `setSaveState('saving')` on edit is kept for instant feedback, but only the store's real sync completion clears it back to `'saved'`. Sessions without cloud sync (`canSyncCloud` false) fall through to `'saved'` immediately, matching the old timer's behavior there since local-only commits are effectively synchronous. Live-verified: two real tabs, same scene, edit + genuine "Saved" (no longer a timer) in tab one, immediate reload of tab two — content now correctly reflects the edit with no staleness, whereas reloading before the fix (during the fake-"Saved" window) reliably reverted it. **(7) After (6), the user reported the symptom again and asked to stop trying to reconcile after the fact — add a warning modal instead when a record is already open for editing in another tab.** This is a deliberate scope change, not a 7th silent-overwrite root cause: proactively warning *before* the edit happens sidesteps the whole class of race the last six fixes were chasing, since it doesn't matter how good the merge/reconciliation logic is if the user simply doesn't start a second concurrent edit in the first place. New `src/utils/useTabPresence.js` — a `BroadcastChannel('yow-record-presence')` presence signal (separate from the storage-sync channel), keyed by an arbitrary string (`scene:${id}` for now), reporting how many *other* tabs currently have that key "active"; a `hello`/`heartbeat`/`bye` protocol with a 10s staleness prune handles tabs that close without cleanly unmounting. Wired into `SceneEditor.jsx`: presence is active only while the scene's real textarea is focused (not merely visible/scrolled-past — matches the actual risk window), and the first time another tab's presence is seen during a focus session, `EditingElsewhereWarning` (new, `src/components/shared/EditingElsewhereWarning.jsx`, reuses the shared `Modal`) blocks with "Go back" (defocuses, returns to preview) or "Edit anyway" (dismisses, no further nagging until the next focus session). Found and fixed one bug in the process of wiring this up: `Modal`/`StudioSheet` focuses itself on mount for accessibility, which blurs the scene's textarea as a side effect the instant the warning appears — "Edit anyway" was initially leaving the user dropped back into read-only preview instead of still editing, since the real `<textarea>` unmounts entirely while unfocused (`focused ? <textarea> : <preview>`) and the stale ref pointed at a detached node; fixed by re-focusing on the *fresh* node (looked up by class after a re-render) rather than trusting `textareaRef`. Scoped to scenes for now, since that's the specific surface with a live, reproduced track record across seven rounds — the presence hook is generic and reusable for structured records (characters/locations/etc.) if warranted later. Live-verified with two real tabs: opening the same scene for editing in tab two while tab one has it focused shows the warning immediately; "Go back" returns to preview; "Edit anyway" restores real editable focus and typing afterward doesn't re-trigger it; opening when nobody else is editing shows nothing. Lint clean, full suite (324 tests) green. |
| 2026-08-01: New browser/device replayed the "create a project or try the sample" first-run modal and all onboarding tours for established accounts | Onboarding UX regression. `App.jsx`'s first-run check (`userProjects`) explicitly excluded sample projects, so an account whose only synced project was the sample showed `0` "real" projects and re-triggered the first-run choice modal on login. Separately, every onboarding/tour "seen" flag (`useTourStore.js`) lived only in a single `localStorage` key with no backend copy, so a brand-new browser or device had no way to know an established account had already dismissed the welcome tour, the wizard, or any of the 14 per-section tours — they all replayed from scratch. | Fixed | Browser-QA: on an account with only a sample project (no other novels), log in from a browser with empty localStorage and confirm the library loads directly with no first-run modal. Separately, on an established account that has completed tours, clear localStorage (or use a new browser/device) and confirm the welcome tour and per-section tours do not replay. | `firstRunChoiceOpen` in `App.jsx` now checks `store.novels.length === 0` (any synced project, including the sample, counts) instead of filtering sample projects out. `useTourStore.js` gained an optional `remoteFlags`/`onPersist` hydration+persistence hook; `App.jsx` wires it to the account's `user_metadata.tour_progress` via the existing `updateProfile` path, so durable `welcome_*`/`wizard_*`/`tour_*` flags sync to the account and hydrate a fresh browser instead of defaulting to "never seen." Verified directly in-browser: completing a section tour wrote the flag to `user_metadata.tour_progress` in Supabase, and a simulated fresh-browser reload (cleared localStorage, session preserved) loaded straight to the library with no first-run modal. Full replay-suppression check on reload was blocked by an unrelated, already-tracked issue (the `scenes.novel_id` migration two rows below not yet applied to the local dev DB caused a data-load error); not part of this fix. `vitest run` (77 relevant tests) and lint pass with no new warnings. |
| 2026-08-01: Free-project selection after losing paid access did not become the dashboard active project | Payment/access consistency bug. The downgrade/free-plan selector saved `user_metadata.free_project_id` and updated the editable `activeNovelId`, but the Projects dashboard hero/star uses the separate project `focus` flag. A stale paid-era focused project could therefore remain visually active on the dashboard even though a different project had been locked as the free editable project. | Fixed | Browser-QA a real downgrade or forced free-plan account with multiple projects: choose a free active project, confirm the modal closes, and verify the Projects dashboard hero/star shows that same project while other projects are view-only. | Store import now treats `freeProjectId` as authoritative over stale account settings/local last-active markers and normalizes the dashboard `focus` flag to the locked free project. The free selector now calls a combined dashboard-active selection path and returns the app shell to the Projects dashboard context. Regression tests in `src/store/useStore.test.js` cover import-time downgrade state, fresher local snapshots, and live `freeProjectId` transition. Focused store suite passed (`47` tests); lint has `0` errors with existing warnings. |
| 2026-08-01: Deleting a project could leave orphaned `scenes` rows in Supabase | Data safety / incomplete delete, not data loss. The `scenes` table was a pre-migration table (see `20260626_normalized_storage.sql`) that never got the `novel_id` column every other novel-scoped table has. Bulk project-delete cleanup (`deleteItemsByNovel` in `src/utils/firestoreSync.js`) filters every `NOVEL_TABLES` entry by `WHERE novel_id = ...`, which silently errored/no-opped for `scenes` (column didn't exist) — the error was swallowed, so it looked like it worked. In practice, cloud scene rows were still being cleaned up via a separate per-scene loop in `deleteNovel` (`src/store/useStore.js`) that deletes by `scene_id` using the in-memory `scenesRef`, so this wasn't observed as active data loss, but that fallback silently depended on `scenesRef` being fully hydrated — any gap there (stale state, a scene that failed to load) would have left orphaned rows with no bulk cleanup net. **2026-08-02 escalation:** the migration below was written and committed alongside client code that immediately started sending `novel_id` on every scene upsert, but the migration itself was never pushed to production. Result: every single scene content save to Supabase had been failing outright with `Could not find the 'novel_id' column of 'scenes' in the schema cache` since that deploy — reproduced live via a user report of "rapidly typed, left the page, came back (still there), refreshed, text disappeared." This is more severe than the original delete-cleanup framing: it meant zero cloud backup for scene edits app-wide, with only the local browser cache (IndexedDB-backed, see `src/storage/browserVaultAdapter.js`) protecting against loss on refresh — fine for normal use, but a real gap for a different device/browser, cleared site data, or browser storage eviction. | Fixed and deployed to production 2026-08-02 | Migration applied directly to the linked production DB (`supabase db query --linked -f supabase/migrations/20260801_scenes_add_novel_id.sql`, since `supabase db push` was blocked by an unrelated pre-existing local/remote migration-history mismatch around the 2026-07-28 migrations — needs separate `supabase migration repair` + `db pull` reconciliation, not done here) followed by `NOTIFY pgrst, 'reload schema'`. Verified column exists (`information_schema.columns`) and did a live end-to-end round-trip: typed into a real scene, confirmed no console error, confirmed the row landed in `public.scenes` with the correct `novel_id` via direct query. Test content cleaned up afterward (cloud row reset via SQL; local edit cleared via the UI). | Chose "add `novel_id` via migration" over "delete scenes by ID only," since it makes `scenes` consistent with every other novel-scoped table and makes bulk cleanup authoritative from the cloud row itself rather than depending on local in-memory state. `saveSceneDoc` and the bulk `getTableRows('scenes', …)` path now write `novel_id` on every save; `deleteItemsByNovel` no longer special-cases `scenes` (dead code removed); the redundant per-scene delete loop in `deleteNovel` (`useStore.js`) was removed since bulk cleanup now covers scenes like every other table. Proven via new tests in `src/utils/firestoreSync.test.js` (`scene cloud cleanup on project delete`): one asserts `saveSceneDoc` writes `novel_id`, the other seeds a mock `scenes` table across two projects/two users and asserts `deleteItemsByNovel` removes only the target project's rows, leaving everything else untouched. Full suite (`vitest run`, 302 tests) passes. Separate, still-open item: the pre-existing migration-history drift around 2026-07-28 (local has a migration remote doesn't, and vice versa) that blocked `supabase db push` should get a proper `migration repair`/`db pull` pass so normal `db push` works again instead of requiring manual `db query -f` for future migrations. |
| 2026-07-29: Sample-project ("Tour with a sample") content can be stranded in local browser storage only | Data safety / broken save. Found while investigating a "30 signups, no returns" report — pulled real signup data from `auth.users`/`novels`/`scenes` and found two real signups with real scene-writing activity (64 and 1 scenes) but zero rows in `novels`, meaning their project record never reached the cloud. Root cause: `ensureSampleProject()` in `useStore.js` wrote the entire seeded demo world (project, ~40 scenes/chapters, characters, locations, lore, etc.) to local state/localStorage only, and explicitly pushed just the single opening scene to Supabase (`saveSceneDoc`) — everything else depended on generic per-collection debounced sync effects that need the account's cloud sync to have already finished initializing, and can silently miss the write if it lands before then. A second instance of the same gap existed in `enrichSampleProject()` (upgrades an older sparse sample to the richer seed content): it bulk-mutates the `scenes` array via `commitLocal`, but `scenes` isn't covered by the generic per-collection sync effects (each scene normally syncs individually as it's edited), so the upgraded content had no sync path to the cloud at all. Net effect: someone taking the sample-tour path (the first, most prominently featured onboarding choice) could explore a fully-populated world that exists only in that one browser — gone with no warning on another device, a private window, or any local-storage eviction, which plausibly explains part of the near-zero return rate for exactly the users who engaged the most. | Fixed and deployed 2026-07-29 (`dc8ba67` pushed to `main`); needs live-account QA | QA: on the deployed production build, choose "Tour with a sample," wait a couple seconds, then query the real Supabase `novels`/`scenes`/`characters` tables directly and confirm the full seeded project (not just one scene) is present; separately, trigger the `enrichSampleProject` upgrade path on an older sparse sample and confirm the upgraded scene content also lands in Supabase, not just localStorage. | Both functions now push their full write (`upsertItems` per table, mirroring the existing proven "local is fresher, push everything" pattern already used elsewhere in `useStore.js`'s `importData`) immediately instead of relying on the debounced effects. `npm run build`-equivalent (`vitest run src/store/useStore.test.js`, 44 tests) passes; local offline-mode dev build boots with no console errors. Pushed to `main` 2026-07-29 for Vercel auto-deploy; not yet verified against the live production Supabase — see QA column. Related: new GA4 event tracking (`src/utils/analytics.js`, `sign_up`/`login`/`onboarding_*` events) was added in the same pass so future funnel drop-off is visible going forward instead of requiring a manual SQL pull. |
| 2026-07-28: Vercel Hobby deploy exceeded Serverless Function limit | Deployment blocked. Vercel counted handler test files colocated under `api/` as Serverless Functions, pushing the deployment over the Hobby plan's 12-function cap. | Fixed | Redeploy from GitHub/Vercel and confirm the deployment passes the function-count phase. | Moved API handler tests from `api/*.test.js` to `tests/api/*.test.js`, updated imports and Vitest include config. `api/` now contains only the 8 real production endpoints. |
| 2026-07-25 user report: Character add/edit modal can close mid-edit without saving | Broken save/data safety risk for character profiles. User reported multiple cases where the character editor disappeared during an edit before saving. | Closed — QA-verified 2026-08-28 | No further action needed. | Added `closeOnBackdrop` to the shared Studio modal wrapper and disabled backdrop-close specifically for the Character add/edit modal. Also added a Pronouns profile field in the character form/detail header, DOCX/PDF exports, and AI context prompts, with store regression coverage for saving/updating the field. **2026-08-28 QA verification** (live browser, `VITE_OFFLINE_MODE=true`, Create Character modal, typed a name + pronouns into every path): backdrop click no longer closes the editor or loses the typed data (modal stays open, field value retained); Escape, the form's own Cancel button, and the header X button all correctly route through the shared unsaved-changes prompt ("Save changes? / Save / Discard / Cancel") instead of closing silently; choosing "Cancel" on that prompt keeps the editor open with data intact; "Discard" closes without creating a character; "Save" from the X-triggered prompt persists the character and closes; the direct "Save Character" submit button persists immediately with the pronouns field saved correctly ("ze/hir" shown on the saved dossier); a full page reload afterward shows both genuinely-saved characters still present and neither of the discarded/cancelled ones reappearing. `npm run lint` (0 errors) and the full `vitest run` suite (409 tests) pass. |
| 2026-07-19 QA: worldbuilding and schedule writes can be lost on immediate refresh | Data safety / broken save. Character create/edit, location create, lore create, timeline event create, and schedule event edits only persisted after waiting roughly 2 seconds in manual QA. Character Builder sheet data was also initially lost after accidental logout/re-login in a D&D project. | Closed 2026-07-22 — confirmed on the live production deployment with the real account | No further action needed. | 2026-07-22 production confirmation pass: on the deployed `https://www.yourownworld.co.uk` build (not local dev), signed in with the real test account, created a new character in an existing project, immediately did a full hard page reload (no wait for the debounce), and confirmed the character was still present after reload — matches the dev-server behavior already verified 2026-07-21. Test character deleted afterward to leave the account clean. This closes the recommended deploy-specific confirmation gap (env vars, CDN caching, etc. could not have masked the fix). 2026-07-19 fix: `loadUserData()` now reports real remote freshness from `updated_at` instead of `Date.now()`; active project selection and affected local records persist synchronously before cloud debounce. Follow-up fix: owner-scoped `nf_lastActiveProject` marker prefers the user's latest local project selection over stale account settings when the project exists. 2026-07-21 live retest (user reproduced: created two Party characters, signed out, neither was there on login) found two distinct bugs specific to `rpgCharacters` (Party/Character Builder): (1) `importData` in `useStore.js` — the login/reconciliation path — restored every other synced entity (`characters`, `factions`, `locations`, etc.) from loaded data but never called `setRpgCharacters(...)` at all, so Party characters reset to empty on every fresh login regardless of whether the cloud write had succeeded; confirmed via direct Supabase REST query that several of the user's own past "lost" characters (including un-renamed "New Adventurer" rows) were sitting in the `rpg_characters` table the whole time. (2) Separately, signing out immediately after a create didn't wait for the entity's 2s debounced cloud push before `supabase.auth.signOut()` revoked the session and the store wiped local storage — added `flushPendingSync()` (`createKeyedDebounce.flushAll()` / `debounce.flush()`) called from `AuthContext.signOut` via a small registry (`src/store/syncFlushRegistry.js`) so any still-debounced writes send with a valid session before sign-out proceeds (4s timeout guard so a slow/offline network can't hang sign-out). (3) Fixing (1) surfaced a third, pre-existing bug: once real cloud data actually reached the Party page, some existing characters in the user's account (e.g. "Mira Thornhand", "Orren Vale" — likely from an older app version or an incomplete AI import) had no `hp` object at all, and `CharacterSheet`/`CharacterBuilder` read `character.hp.current` directly with no guard, crashing the whole Party page with "Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'current')" the moment those records loaded. Added `normalizeRpgCharacter()` in `rpgData.js` that backfills missing `hp`/`abilityScores`/`currency`/`spells` from `makeNewCharacter()`'s defaults without touching fields the record already has; applied wherever `rpgCharacters` is exposed from the store and when saving new characters. (4) The (3) fix only healed rpgCharacters at the point they're read/rendered — it never fixed the actual bad row in Supabase, so "Mira Thornhand" and "Orren Vale" would need re-healing in memory on every single login forever, and any other client reading the row directly (a future second client, a direct DB query) would still hit the crash. Root cause: `importData` normalizes and sets the healed characters into state, but the regular debounced cloud-sync effect that would otherwise push that change is suppressed for the whole import (`importing.current`, cleared 500ms after import finishes) and nothing changes `rpgCharacters` again afterward to re-trigger it — so the healed write was silently dropped. Fixed by explicitly diffing healed vs. raw characters in `importData` and pushing only the ones that actually changed via `upsertItems('rpg_characters', ...)` in the same settle-timeout that clears `importing.current`. Live-verified against the real Supabase table: before the fix, Mira/Orren's rows had no `hp` key and a `updated_at` hours old; after, both rows show `hp: {max:10,current:10,temp:0}` with `updated_at` matching the exact login that triggered the heal. (5) Unrelated bug noticed in passing and also fixed: the character-creation wizard's name field started pre-filled with the literal text "New Adventurer" (not a placeholder) with no auto-select-on-focus, so clicking in and typing without first clearing produced names like "New AdventurerAragorn". Fixed by starting the wizard's draft with an empty name (`makeNewCharacter(novelId, { name: '' })`) — the wizard already blocks advancing past step 1 until a non-empty name is entered, so this doesn't weaken validation, it just stops the field from pre-poisoning itself. `makeNewCharacter`'s default name is untouched for other creation paths (e.g. AI import) that don't go through this form. All five fixes verified live end-to-end: created a character, signed out within ~1s, logged back in, character present and in the active party; opened the previously-crashing "Test 1 - D&D Campaign" Party page and Mira Thornhand's full sheet, both render correctly; confirmed via direct Supabase REST query that the healed rows are now actually fixed in the database, not just papered over client-side; created a character by clicking straight into the name field and typing without clearing it first, got a clean "Aragorn" with no concatenation. `npm run build` also verified clean (no bundler-level issue from the new cross-module import). Tests: `src/utils/firestoreSync.test.js`, `src/store/useStore.test.js` (`flushPendingSync` describe block, `restores rpg (Party) characters with imported project data`, `backfills a missing hp object on rpg characters read from storage, and heals it in state`, `pushes a healed rpg character back to the cloud after import settles`). |
| 2026-07-19 QA: two open tabs silently overwrite scene edits | Data loss. Editing the same project in two tabs caused the later tab's version to replace earlier tab edits with no conflict warning or merge path. | QA passed 2026-07-19; keep in regression | Keep covered in release regression: write in tab A, wait for save, write different text in stale tab B, refresh/reopen, and verify tab A's newer text survives as a scene titled with `(conflict copy)` rather than disappearing. | 2026-07-19 fix: stale scene saves compare against the persisted browser copy; if the persisted scene is newer and content differs, the newer text is preserved as a conflict-copy scene before the stale write lands. Unit coverage added in `src/store/useStore.test.js`. |
| 2026-07-19: Editing a scene could wipe scene text across every other project after a stale localStorage write | Data loss, recurring. The two-tab conflict-copy fix above rebuilt the *entire* in-memory scenes array from the browser's persisted `nf_scenes` snapshot rather than live app state whenever that snapshot was non-empty; `save()` silently swallowed localStorage write failures (e.g. `QuotaExceededError`) with only a console warning. Reproduced on the QA test account after populating 25 projects: deleting/editing scenes left scene text missing across all projects in the app. A second, deeper layer of the same defect kept the bug recurring even after the first fix: `nf_localWriteAt` (used by `importData` to decide whether to trust the local browser copy over the cloud on login/reload) advances on *every* `commitLocal` call regardless of which key it was for, so a healthy small write (e.g. a chapter rename) kept making a stale, quota-failed `nf_scenes` snapshot look "fresher than the cloud," and the app kept reloading the same broken local copy. Supabase cloud data was never affected; only logging out (which clears all local keys) reliably cleared the poisoned state, and the bug would return once local storage filled up again. | Fixed 2026-07-19, needs regression QA | Regression-test: populate several projects with enough content to approach the browser storage cap, edit/delete scenes across projects, refresh/relogin without logging out first, and confirm no other project's live text is ever replaced; keep the existing two-tab conflict-copy test green; confirm the new storage-full toast appears/clears correctly and links to Storage settings. | `mergeSceneUpdateWithPersistedCopy` now only reads the persisted localStorage copy to detect a stale-tab conflict for the single scene being edited — the rebuilt array is always based on live state (`prev`), never the localStorage snapshot. `save()` now tracks per-key write failures in `nf_localWriteFailed`; `importData`'s "prefer local over cloud" decision refuses to trust the local snapshot at all while any key has a known failed write, so a fresh reconciliation now self-heals without requiring logout. Added a dismissible in-app warning (`store.localStorageWarning`, polled from the same failure flag) surfaced via `membership-toast` in `App.jsx` when local storage is failing to write, linking to Storage settings. Cross-tab conflict-copy protection (row above) is unchanged and still covered by `src/store/useStore.test.js`. |
| 2026-07-19 QA: D&D/TTRPG promised session workflow missing or novel-framed | Final-launch promise gap. Campaign projects show Arc/Session/Encounter labels, but structured prep/recap fields were not discoverable, AI prompt context and overview language remained novel-like, and progress/word-target copy did not fit campaign planning. | Dashboard/hero language, Sessions prep/recap panel, and D&D-vs-system-neutral copy live-QA'd 2026-07-21 and all passed with no bugs found; AI-behavior live QA still open | Remaining: live-QA project-type-aware AI behavior (chat/compact actions/manuscript suggestions/specialist tools framing D&D as DM-side and TTRPG as GM-side system-neutral) and confirm the bottom AI bar's chat history persists per-project — not exercised this pass. | 2026-07-19 slices: shared project stats now derive campaign sessions, encounters, prep coverage, recap coverage, and uncapped session-target progress; D&D/TTRPG dashboard and active-project hero use those stats instead of novel word-target completion; campaign settings and welcome/create flows use `sessionTarget` with a 12-session default; dashboard states Arc/Session/Encounter; Sessions now surface structured prep/recap fields on session headings and write `sessionPlan`/`sessionRecap` for stats/export; template filtering has active-type coverage; shared AI prompt context now covers Novel, Novella, Short Story, D&D Campaign, TTRPG Campaign, and Comic/Graphic Novel across full chat, compact assistant actions, manuscript suggestions, and specialist AI tools; bottom AI bar prompts/responses now save into the overall per-project chat history. Tests: `src/utils/projectStats.test.js`, `src/components/Manuscript/ManuscriptCampaignWorkflow.test.jsx`, `src/components/Manuscript/manuscriptTemplates.test.js`, `src/utils/aiToolPrompts.test.js`, `src/utils/aiChatHistory.test.js`. 2026-07-21 live QA (real account, real Supabase, both `Test 1 - D&D Campaign` and `Test 1 - TTRPG Campaign`): dashboard/hero for both use "CAMPAIGN PROGRESS" with Sessions Planned/Encounters/Prep %, "X sessions and Y encounters planned," "N with prep · N with recap," and the correct planning-structure line — no novel word-target language found anywhere. D&D shows "DM campaign plan · Story Arc -> Session -> Encounter"; TTRPG shows "GM campaign plan · Campaign Arc -> Session -> Encounter" and its Shape line explicitly reads "System-neutral campaign bible. Works for any ruleset, tone, or genre — fantasy, horror, sci-fi, or homebrew," with no D&D-specific terms leaking in (different session-beat labels too: Hook/Score/Fallout for TTRPG vs. Opening Encounter/Development/Boss Fight for D&D). Opened the Sessions view for D&D and confirmed the "Session Prep & Recap" panel is directly visible under each encounter heading with all documented fields (Prep: Hooks, Encounter flow, NPCs, Rewards, Consequences, Session notes; Recap: Recap, Player choices, Fallout, Next hooks). Typed into the Hooks field, confirmed the value persisted correctly in both `localStorage` (`chapter.sessionPlan.hooks`) and the real Supabase `chapters` table after a full page reload, then reverted the test value in both places. |
| 2026-07-19 QA: manuscript structure rename/delete/reorder/move is incomplete | Core editor usability. Manual QA found unclear/missing structure rename controls, inconsistent custom title display, delete behavior concerns, reorder persistence concerns, and no move path into empty parents. | Fixed 2026-08-25 — explicit move control implemented, code-reviewed, and tested. Row closed; no further action. | None. | 2026-07-19 structure-control slice added explicit act/chapter/scene rename controls, preserved custom titles that start with the structure label, and enabled dragging chapters/scenes into empty acts/chapters. Tests: `src/components/Manuscript/StructureSidebar.test.jsx`. 2026-07-21 investigation + fix: code review of `deleteAct`/`deleteChapter`/`deleteScene` in `useStore.js` found the cascade-delete and confirmation-dialog logic (both in `StructureSidebar.jsx` and `StoryOutline.jsx`, the latter showing real descendant counts) is correct — no bug found there. Reorder/move persistence, however, had a real gap: acts and chapters sync to the cloud automatically via a blanket `debouncedSaveItems` effect on their arrays, but scenes sync individually via `debouncedSaveScene`/`saveSceneDoc`, and `reorderScene`/`moveScene` never called either — every same-browser refresh looked fine (localStorage was always correct) but the new scene order/chapter placement silently never reached Supabase, so it would revert on a fresh login or another device. Fixed by pushing `debouncedSaveScene` for every scene whose `order`/`chapterId` actually changed in both functions. Unit-tested (`src/store/useStore.test.js`: new `scene reorder/move cloud sync` describe block, asserting the cloud mock is called with the correct final `order`/`chapterId` for every affected scene). Live-verified against the real Supabase database in a scratch test project: dispatched a real HTML5 drag-and-drop reorder in the Structure sidebar, then queried the `scenes` table directly and confirmed both rows' stored `order` matched the new on-screen order (scratch project deleted afterward). 2026-07-22 live-QA of delete confirmation/cascade in the browser (real Supabase account, disposable "Delete Cascade QA Scratch" project, deleted afterward): built Act 1 (Chapter 1 with 2 scenes, Chapter 2 with 1 scene) plus an empty Act 2 in the Structure sidebar. Deleting a single scene removed it immediately with no confirmation prompt (expected — no cascade risk). Deleting a chapter with a scene inside, and deleting an act with a chapter and scene inside, both triggered a native confirm dialog and, once accepted, correctly cascade-removed every descendant in one pass — verified by re-reading the sidebar tree after each delete. Confirmed persistence (not just local state) with a full hard reload from Supabase afterward: the project showed 0 words and the deleted structure did not reappear. Remaining, lower-priority gap (now closed, see below): `StructureSidebar.jsx` only supported moving a scene/chapter into an empty parent via drag-and-drop; `StoryOutline.jsx` already had an explicit "move to…" dropdown that also lists empty parents — 2026-08-25 product decision (asked live via the `product-owner` skill): add the explicit control — more discoverable and better for keyboard/touch than drag-and-drop-only, and keeps the two editors consistent. 2026-08-25 `frontend-engineer` implementation: added an explicit "move to act"/"move to chapter" `<select>` control to `StructureSidebar.jsx`, alongside (not replacing) the existing drag-and-drop, unlike drag-and-drop this can target any parent, populated or not, matching `StoryOutline.jsx`'s existing behavior. The control only renders when there is more than one possible destination (a single-act/single-chapter project has nowhere to move to). During implementation the duplication with `StoryOutline.jsx`'s `ParentSelect` was extracted into a new shared `src/components/shared/ParentMoveSelect.jsx` component (shared behavior — value/options/onChange/aria-label — with per-caller `className` for each sidebar's own visual style), and both call sites now delegate to it. `code-reviewer` (high effort) reviewed the diff and raised two findings, both fixed before landing: (1) moving into a populated parent always appends via `destCount` (count of existing items in the destination) — reviewed against `moveChapter`/`moveScene`'s cloud-sync path (the 2026-07-21 fix logged above) and confirmed this is safe, an append is a well-defined operation with no ordering ambiguity even under concurrent multi-tab edits, and the same append pattern is what `StoryOutline.jsx` already ships; (2) `.ms-sidebar-move-select`'s CSS declared `text-overflow: ellipsis` without the required `overflow: hidden; white-space: nowrap`, so truncation silently did nothing — fixed in `src/index.css`. Verified across project types via `labels.level1`/`level2`/`level3` (sourced from `src/constants/projectTypes.js`, not hardcoded to Novel's Act/Chapter/Scene terms). Tests: 3 new cases added to `src/components/Manuscript/StructureSidebar.test.jsx` (move scene into a populated chapter, move chapter into another act, control absent when there is only one destination); 2 pre-existing tests updated to disambiguate `getByText` matches against the new `<option>` elements. Full suite green: `npm run lint` (0 errors; only pre-existing warnings) and `npm run test` (408/408 passed). |
| 2026-07-19 QA: mobile writing area is too narrow | Responsive blocker. On mobile the writing area used only a few words of width despite the roadmap requirement that the core writing flow use the available viewport. | Closed 2026-07-22 — bottom tab bar/overlay reachability also passed | 2026-07-19 writing-surface slice removes mobile document side borders/shadow, tightens manuscript padding, keeps the editor at full viewport width, and constrains the formatting panel/tool sheet within the viewport. 2026-07-20: live-verified `.manuscript-document` renders at 91% of a 375px viewport with `max-width: none`, no fixed narrow column. Also improved the font-size slider (12-30px): the 960px column max-width now scales proportionally with font size instead of staying flat, so larger text still gets a sensible line length instead of wasting the extra width the page has. 2026-07-22 live QA (real production account, 375px viewport, "QA 13 - Novel" manuscript): confirmed the last open item from [docs/QA_PLAN.md](QA_PLAN.md) — the mobile bottom tab bar (Outline/Status/Notes/Format/AI) stays visible and reachable at all times; tapping a tab opens its panel as a bounded overlay over (not instead of) the manuscript, tapping the active tab again closes it and cleanly reveals the manuscript text underneath at full width with no leftover clipping or overlap; switched between the Outline and Format panels and confirmed each renders fully scrollable with every control (font family, size, spacing, alignment, indent) reachable and tappable, none clipped or hidden behind the tab bar. No mobile writing-area gaps remain open. |
| 2026-07-25 user report: mobile app feels over-zoomed, layered, and shows a white bar at top/bottom on iPhone | Responsive/polish blocker. Three distinct reports: (1) manuscript text rendered far too large and not using the full screen width; (2) writing screen felt like stacked overlays with no clear way to see/close back to what was behind; (3) a white strip appeared at the top and bottom of the screen (notch/home-indicator area) on iPhone Safari. | Fixed, needs real-device iOS QA for item 3 | Root-caused via local mobile-viewport reproduction, not just code review. (1) `ManuscriptZoomControl`'s page-zoom preference (`nf-focused-writing:<user>` in localStorage, 80–150%) is shared across desktop and mobile with no clamp; a zoom set comfortably on a desktop screen (e.g. 150%) was reproduced live to make the mobile `.manuscript-document` render oversized exactly as described. Fixed by forcing `zoom: 1 !important` on `.manuscript-document` and hiding `.ms-focus-zoom` inside the existing `@media (max-width: 640px)` blocks in `src/index.css` (mobile already has its own font-size control in the Format panel, so page-zoom is now desktop-only) — verified live with a 150%-zoomed profile that mobile now renders at normal size while desktop keeps the 150% control working. (2) The writing sidebar (`Manuscript.jsx`'s `activeSidebarTab`) defaulted to `'structure'` open on mount; on desktop that's a persistent side panel, but at ≤640px the same sidebar becomes a full bottom-sheet overlay (`ms-writing-sidebar` in `src/index.css`), so every mobile visit landed with the Outline sheet already covering the manuscript. Fixed by defaulting to closed (`null`) when `window.innerWidth <= 640` at mount; the existing tap-active-tab-to-close and the panel's own "Close panel" ✕ both already worked correctly once the tour overlay wasn't intercepting the tap — verified live that Outline now opens and closes cleanly on tap, landing on the manuscript text by default. (3) No `viewport-fit=cover` or `theme-color` meta existed, so iOS Safari couldn't extend the page's own themed background under the status-bar/home-indicator safe areas and fell back to its own (white) chrome there — the standard cause of this exact symptom. Added `viewport-fit=cover` to `index.html`'s viewport meta, added a `theme-color` meta tag, and made `applyThemeToDocument` (`src/utils/theme.js`) keep it in sync with the active theme's `bgMain` on every theme change so the browser chrome always matches (light and dark built-in themes, custom, and quick-palette). `body` now pads for `env(safe-area-inset-top/bottom)` so its themed background (not white) fills the safe area, and `.studio-shell` moved to `height: 100dvh` (was `100vh`) with `padding-top: env(safe-area-inset-top)` at ≤860px to remove the toolbar-show/hide gap. Item 3 cannot be confirmed from this sandboxed environment (no real iOS Safari) — needs a live-device check per the instructions below. `npm run build` and `npm run lint`-equivalent both clean; no new console errors in the reproduction. 2026-07-25 follow-up (user-supplied screenshot of the unpatched production build, narrow desktop-Safari-in-dev-mode width, "Mobile" site mode already active — ruling out Desktop-Site as the cause): found and fixed a distinct, higher-severity pre-existing bug the first pass missed — the outer app header's "collapse label to icon-only" rule for `.studio-utility-btn` (Project Settings/Exit/etc. in `Layout.jsx`) was scoped to `@media (max-width: 1400px) and (min-width: 861px)` in `src/index.css`, with a *separate* `@media (max-width: 420px)` block handling only very narrow phones — leaving a real gap from 421–860px (most phones, including any iPhone at 428–430px width such as the Pro Max line, and tablet-portrait widths) where buttons fall through to full desktop text labels with no collapsing at all, causing exactly the "tons of overlap in the top bar" the user reported. Fixed by removing the `min-width: 861px` floor so the icon-only collapse now covers the full 0–1400px range (the later, more specific ≤420px block still applies its own tighter sizing on top for very small phones — no conflict, verified via cascade/build). While root-causing this, also found and fixed an adjacent gap in the same code path: `.ms-toolbar` (the manuscript Template/Import/Finalise/Search/etc. row) wraps correctly ≤640px but had no wrap or scroll behavior for 641–1400px, so on tablet-portrait widths it silently overflowed and rendered behind the sidebar rail instead of wrapping/scrolling; added `overflow-x: auto` as a safe fallback. Both re-verified live at 375px, 430px (the exact previously-broken width), 600px, and 700px with no overlap and the toolbar properly wrapping/scrolling — `npm run build` clean. This pass also corrected an earlier miscommunication: none of this branch's fixes are committed or deployed, so every screenshot the user takes reflects the current unpatched `origin/main` production build regardless of local session changes; still needs both a commit/deploy and the real-device Safari safe-area check above before closing. 2026-07-25 second follow-up (user testing the local dev build directly via browser dev tools, explicit ask: "fully optimised for editing/typing on a mobile phone", not just "not broken"): audited the actual typing experience, not just layout, and found the mobile writing screen buried the manuscript under real chrome even after the fixes above — confirmed live by actually typing a test sentence into a scene at 390px width. Fixed three concrete things: (1) Focused writing mode (`FocusedWritingShell`, entered via the toolbar's "Focus" button) is this app's purpose-built distraction-reduced editor state, but on mobile it still rendered the full outer app header (logo/Write/Settings/Exit/Account, `.studio-spine`) above its own compact topbar, defeating the point — added `.studio-shell:has(.manuscript-processor.is-focused-writing) .studio-spine { display: none }` plus a matching `grid-template-rows` collapse, scoped to `@media (max-width: 780px)`, so focused mode now reclaims that ~90px and is genuinely full-screen on phones (`Exit focus` still returns to the normal header). (2) The manuscript toolbar's Notes and AI buttons open the exact same panel as the Notes/AI tabs already in the bottom tab strip — pure duplication on a narrow screen; hidden both on ≤640px (`ms-toolbar-btn-notes`/`ms-toolbar-btn-ai` classes added in `Manuscript.jsx`, hidden with `!important` in `index.css` since the base `.ms-toolbar-btn` display rule sits later in the file and otherwise wins on source order — verified live after first finding the cascade bug via computed-style inspection). (3) The Act/Chapter divider spacing (`mt-16`/`pt-14 pb-8`, ~56-64px each, sized for a desktop "page break" feel) forced significant scrolling past decorative whitespace before reaching the actual text on every scene open; tightened to 28px/24px/12px on mobile only. Also root-caused and ruled out a suspected "tapping the manuscript text doesn't focus the editor" bug — confirmed via direct DOM inspection that `.ms-preview`'s `onClick={activate}` correctly swaps in the real `<textarea>` and autosave/word-count update normally; the false alarm was this sandbox's remote-browser click-coordinate translation, not an app defect, so real touch taps are expected to work. Live-verified end-to-end at 390px and 1280px: typed a full sentence, saw live word count and autosave, confirmed focused mode now shows only its own compact topbar + bottom tab strip with no outer chrome. `npm run build` clean. Still not deployed — same caveat as above. 2026-07-25 third follow-up (user testing the local dev build directly, reported "can't reach the dismiss button for the browser storage warning", plus "new project/new series/import should all be one row" and "minimise line spacing and margins on mobile manuscript editor"): reproduced all three live. (1) `.membership-toast` (the "browser storage full" / read-only / local-mode toasts in `App.jsx`, `src/index.css`) is `position: fixed; bottom: 18px; z-index: 100`, with no mobile awareness at all — on the manuscript screen the bottom writing-tab-strip sits at `z-index: 200`, so it rendered on top of and completely hid the toast's Dismiss button (confirmed via `elementFromPoint` before/after); other bottom bars (focused-mode controls) go up to `z-index: 250`, so a small bump wouldn't have been robust. Fixed by raising the toast to `z-index: 9000`, this codebase's existing convention for must-see overlays (cookie banner, global search, version history modal), and adding `env(safe-area-inset-bottom)` to its bottom offset; re-verified live that Dismiss is now on top of and clickable over the tab strip. (2) The library's New Project/New Series/Import row (`.library-action-cluster`) allowed wrapping and needed ~289px for three buttons inside a ~240px column once the account controls' reserved width was subtracted — measured live, then fixed by setting `flex-wrap: nowrap` and shrinking the buttons' mobile padding/font (10px/10px → 7px/9px) so all three fit one row within the actual available width, not just an assumed one. (3) The mobile line-spacing/margin request led to the real underlying bug: `SceneEditor.jsx` applies the Format panel's `fontSize`/`lineHeight` as an inline `style` (`textStyle`), which beats any stylesheet rule regardless of specificity — the previous mobile `line-height: 1.72` CSS override (from the first follow-up above) was silently never applying, and the true rendered value was the Format default of `lineHeight: 2` (double-spaced) confirmed via computed-style inspection. Separately, `.ms-preview`/`.ms-textarea` were also carrying an unrelated `padding: 34px 42px 36px 66px` from a shared base rule seemingly meant for a different (lined-paper-styled) editor, adding a lopsided 66px left inset on top of `.manuscript-document`'s own 14px page margin. Fixed both with `!important` overrides scoped to `@media (max-width: 640px)` — `line-height: 1.45` and `padding: 6px 0` — since `!important` in an author stylesheet is the one thing that can beat an inline style; live-verified by typing a multi-sentence paragraph and comparing before/after (line count for the same text dropped substantially, text now starts at the page's own margin instead of an extra 66px in). `npm run build` clean throughout. Still not deployed. 2026-07-25 fourth follow-up ("apply the same logic throughout all areas... minimise unnecessary blank space" — a general mobile-density pass beyond the manuscript, done by auditing live-rendered screens rather than guessing): (1) Library dashboard's "Library Snapshot" stat row (`.active-project-command-stats`/`--compact`) was hard-forced to `grid-template-columns: 1fr` on mobile, stacking all 3 tiles (Projects/Series/Total words) into 3 full-width rows regardless of how few tiles there were; changed to `repeat(auto-fit, minmax(90px, 1fr))` so 3 small tiles share one row and only genuinely-cramped cases wrap — same fix point also serves this pattern anywhere else it's reused. (2) Project overview's "Writing Progress" card had a fixed `min-height: 292px` and a 58.5px "0" (measured live) for what's often a single stat, plus its 80,000/0% pair was rendering at their `clamp()` max (40px) in a 139px-wide column, pressing "80,000" right up against "0%" with no gap. Fixed by letting the card size to content on mobile and reducing both number sizes (58.5px→40px, 40px→26px); re-verified live that the numbers no longer crowd each other and the card is now roughly half its previous height with real content. Both fixes are appended in a new `@media (max-width: 640px)` block at the end of `src/index.css`, deliberately positioned last (this file defines several sections' styles more than once at different points) so they reliably win the cascade, and each was checked against live computed styles rather than assumed from reading the source. This pass covered the two highest-traffic screens (library dashboard, project overview) as a representative sample, not an exhaustive sweep of every screen (worldbuilding sections — Characters/Locations/Lore/Timeline/etc. — account settings, and modals haven't been individually audited yet); flag specific screens that still feel spacious and they can get the same treatment. `npm run build` clean. Still not deployed — same caveat as above. 2026-08-01 fifth follow-up (root-caused a distinct iOS Safari annoyance: any focused text input under 16px triggers Safari's auto-zoom-on-focus, which can leave the page visibly panned/cut off after the field blurs — first caught in `NovelManager.jsx`'s New Series/New Project modal inputs, which were rendering at 13–14px): swept the whole codebase for this pattern rather than patching just the one report. The shared `.field` class (`src/index.css`) intentionally has no base `font-size` — it inherits from whatever the consuming component sets — so any component pairing it with Tailwind's `text-sm`/`text-xs` (14px/12px) or a sub-16px inline `fontSize` produces a real, focusable field that zooms. Found and fixed roughly 150 such fields across ~30 files: eight shared `INPUT`/`IN` style constants used across worldbuilding forms (`Lore.jsx`, `EraManager.jsx`, `ChronicleEntryForm.jsx`, `JourneyBeatModal.jsx`, `CharacterJourneyForm.jsx`, `Characters.jsx`, `Factions.jsx`, `Locations.jsx`) plus their compact search/filter/sort bars; login (`LoginPage.jsx`); the family tree "Add Relative" panel and focus/view selects (`FamilyTree.jsx`); world history, timeline, and relationship map search/selects; the AI chat and character-interview textareas/inputs (`AIPanel.jsx`, `AIAssistant.jsx`, `CharacterInterview.jsx`); the manuscript notes textarea and outline description fields; account settings' vault-restore select and delete-account confirmation input; series creation/assignment; the ideas board's quick-capture, tag, kanban, filter, and convert-to-entity fields; the map builder's shared `inputStyle` constant plus ~15 individual overrides (map name, stamp search, wall material, grid/label settings, location/territory forms); and the biggest concentration in the D&D character sheet/wizard (`CharacterSheet.jsx`, `CharacterBuilder.jsx`, `CharacterWizard.jsx`) — temp HP, equipment, spells, features, backstory/secrets, NPC relationships, and level-up (ASI) fields. Also found two CSS-level instances of the same bug that no per-component grep would catch: `.project-settings-form-grid .field` and `.project-settings-category-editor .field` (both in `src/index.css`, used by `Layout.jsx`'s Project Settings panel — title/description/series/progress/backup/category fields) were hard-set to `font-size: 12px` in the class itself. Fixed all Tailwind cases by swapping `text-sm`/`text-xs` for `text-base` (or removing the override), all inline-style cases by setting `fontSize: 16`, and both CSS rules to `font-size: 16px`. Deliberately left one out-of-scope: `IdeasBoard.jsx`'s sticky-note canvas has a genuinely user-adjustable per-note font size (10–36px, via +/- controls) for visual density on a freeform board — forcing a 16px floor there would fight the intended feature rather than fix an oversight, so it's unchanged (same judgment call this roadmap already made for the manuscript's desktop-only zoom control). `npm run lint` clean (0 errors, only pre-existing warnings) and `npm run build` clean throughout. Not yet verified on a real iOS Safari device — same live-device caveat as the rest of this row. | Engineering/QA — needs a real iPhone Safari pass (safe-area + actual on-screen-keyboard behavior), a deploy before further screenshot-based verification is meaningful, and a wider sweep of remaining screens if the user flags specific ones |
