Novel ZIP backups included comic pages/panels, while the manual expectation was that backup ZIPs remain YOW JSON for restore and readable Word documents should include worldbuilding/project data. | Fixed and live-verified 2026-07-21; category Word ZIP implemented 2026-08-08 | Re-run the QA_PLAN readable-DOCX-bundle checklist against the deployed build as part of the next full QA pass, now expecting a readable ZIP with separate category `.docx` files rather than one combined `.docx`. | Product decision (2026-07-20): the promised readable worldbuilding export is the DOCX bundle (`projectExportDocx.js`, already includes project data, outline/campaign sessions, characters, locations, factions, lore, notes, timeline, world history), not the World Bible PDF/HTML export — that remains a secondary visual/stylised export only. 2026-08-08 product follow-up: the user-facing readable Word export is now one ZIP containing separate category Word files for Overview, Outline/Campaign Sessions/Comic Script, Manuscript/Session Drafts, Schedule, Characters, Locations, Factions, Lore, Notes, Timeline, World History, and AI Chats when present. Engineering fix (2026-07-21): `getProjectExportData` in `useStore.js` only included `comicPages`/`comicPanels` behind the ZIP's own `isComicProject` file-name gate for the two dedicated `data/comic-*.json` files, but the top-level `project-data.json` blob is a verbatim dump of the same object, so non-comic exports still carried `comicPages`/`comicPanels` keys (empty in the ordinary case, populated if stray comic records ever shared a novelId). Fixed by only including those two fields in the returned export object at all when `project.type === 'comic'`, matching the same gate already used for the dedicated per-type files. Unit-tested (`src/store/useStore.test.js`: `getProjectExportData` describe block, covers a non-comic project with a stray comic page attached and confirms a comic project still gets its pages/panels). Full vitest suite (201 tests) and production build both clean. Live-verified in a running local dev build: seeded a stray comic-page record sharing a real Novel project's ID, exported its Backup ZIP by intercepting the actual download Blob and parsing the ZIP's stored (uncompressed) entries in-browser, and confirmed `project-data.json` has no `comicPages`/`comicPanels` keys and the ZIP file listing has no `data/comic-*.json` entries at all. | Do not remove JSON backup files; they are needed for restore. |
| 2026-07-19 QA: internal linking unlink/reverse references unreliable | Worldbuilding reliability issue. Links between characters, lore, locations, timeline, and history did not consistently unlink from both sides. | Two real root causes found and fixed 2026-07-21; unit-tested and live-verified | No further action needed for the two confirmed gaps below. If further QA finds additional cross-reference desyncs (e.g. locations, timeline/history), reopen with the specific entity pair and repro steps. | 2026-07-21 investigation: most "incoming reference" UI (lore↔character/location, timeline↔character/location, character relationship detail chips) is computed live on every render from forward arrays (`characterIds`/`locationIds`/`loreIds`/`linkedCharacters` etc. — see `src/utils/worldLinks.js`), so there was no possible desync there by construction; `deleteLocation`, `deleteEvent`, `deleteHistoryEntry`, `deleteFaction`, and `linkTimelineHistory`/`unlinkTimelineHistory` in `useStore.js` all correctly scrub the relevant arrays on the other side already. Found two real bugs in the two paths that do store a bidirectional/self-referencing array: (1) `deleteCharacter` (`useStore.js`) tried to purge dangling character `relationships` on every other character using `rel.characterId`, but relationship objects only ever use `rel.targetId` (`Characters.jsx`) — the filter was always a no-op, so every other character kept a reference to a deleted character forever (masked in the UI by a `.filter(rel => rel.target)` guard in the detail view). Fixed by correcting the field name. (2) `deleteLoreEntry` never scrubbed the deleted entry's id out of other lore entries' `loreIds` (self-referencing "Related Lore" links) — every other delete path (character/location/timeline) already did this cleanup, lore was the one gap. Fixed by adding the same `commitLocal` scrub pass used elsewhere. Unit-tested (`src/store/useStore.test.js`: new tests in `character CRUD` and a new `lore CRUD` describe block). Live-verified in a running local dev build: seeded two characters with a stored relationship, deleted the referenced character through the real Characters UI, and confirmed via `localStorage` that the surviving character's `relationships` array was correctly emptied (not just masked in the detail view). Full vitest suite (203 tests) and production build both clean. |
| 2026-07-19 QA: AI tools need provider/error/progress UX | AI feature quality issue. Plot Hole, Lore Conflict, and Style tools run too long on a 40k-word project without clear progress/error reporting; no-provider states are missing in some AI tools; findings do not link users to the area to fix. | All seven identified gaps fixed and verified 2026-07-21; no further action needed | None outstanding from this pass. | Investigation on 2026-07-21 found progress UI, no-provider checks, and finding-navigation links already existed for Plot Hole/Lore Conflict/Style (added in an earlier, undocumented commit) — those are working as intended, not gaps. Found and fixed seven real remaining issues instead: (1) AI Import's "Analyze with AI" button was only disabled by `!files.length`, not by a missing provider key, so a user without a key could click it, get an inline error, and plausibly read that as "it appeared to work unexpectedly" — now also disabled when no provider is configured (matching Character Interview's existing pattern), in `AIImportModal.jsx`. (2) Plot Hole/Lore Conflict/Style silently cap manuscript content at 20 scenes and ~600 characters per scene with zero indication to the user — added `getManuscriptCoverage()`/`getManuscriptCoverageForNovel()` in `aiToolPrompts.js` and a shared `ManuscriptCoverageNotice` component that shows e.g. "analysing the first 20 of 32 scenes — 12 scenes will be skipped; some scene text has been shortened to fit" whenever content is being cut. (3) Plot Hole/Lore Conflict/Style were visible but functionally blind for Comic/Graphic Novel projects — they only ever read `store.scenes`, which comics never populate (comics store `comicPages`/`comicPanels` instead), and Style Consistency's Analyse button was hard-disabled by `!scenes.length`, blocking every comic project outright. Added `summariseComicPages()`/`buildManuscriptContentSection()` so all three tools read actual comic page/panel content (title, summary, page-turn, panel description/art notes/dialogue/captions/SFX) for comic projects, and replaced the scene-count gate with a type-aware `hasContent` check plus comic-appropriate copy ("No pages yet" / analyse across "pages"). (4) Bad API key (401/403), rate limits (429), and provider outages (5xx) all rendered through the exact same generic error box with whatever raw string the provider returned — added `friendlyErrorMessage()` in `aiApi.js` mapping status codes to specific, actionable copy ("Check it in AI Settings" for a bad key, "wait a moment and try again" for a rate limit, "this isn't something you can fix" for a provider outage) while keeping the raw message as a parenthetical detail; network failures also got a friendlier lead-in. (5) Character Interview didn't use the shared `useAiRunControls` hook, so a truly stalled request had no timeout and no cancel affordance — wired it in, matching the other three tools, with a Cancel button that appears while streaming. (6) The in-flight indicator was a bare character count with no sense of whether a run was still alive on a long manuscript — added `elapsedMs` tracking to `useAiRunControls` and a shared `AiRunProgress` component with an elapsed-time readout and an indeterminate animated bar (deliberately not a fake percentage, since true progress isn't knowable for LLM streaming without a known expected output length). (7) `FindingCard.jsx`'s `RefLink` rendered a finding's unresolved reference (AI free-text that didn't match any actual entity by name) as plain inert text, indistinguishable from text that was never meant to be linkable — now shown with a muted dashed underline and a tooltip explaining the reference couldn't be matched. Unit-tested throughout (`aiToolPrompts.test.js`, new `aiApi.test.js`, new `useAiRunControls.test.js`, `FindingCard.test.jsx`); full vitest suite (224 tests) and production build clean. Live-verified in a running local dev build against a real 32-scene project (confirmed the exact truncation-notice wording and a working progress bar/elapsed-time/cancel run) and a real comic project (confirmed Style Analysis's button is no longer disabled and Plot Hole correctly shows "Ready to analyse" reading comic page/panel content instead of an empty scenes section). |

Could be a real focused-mode regression or a flaky comfort-zone assertion; CI has not exercised these since 2026-06-30 because of the lint failure above. | Fixed 2026-07-21, full 68-test suite passing | No further action needed. | 2026-07-21 triage found one test bug and one real app bug. Test bug: the "keeps tools available" assertion used `getByText('...', {exact:true})` for the project title, which matched both the studio nav banner's `<h1>` (stays visible by design in focused mode) and the focused topbar's own `<strong class="ms-focus-project-title">` — scoped the locator to `.ms-focus-project-title` specifically. Real bug: `useCaretComfortScroll` tries to keep the caret within the container's 35–65% band by scrolling, but `.manuscript-processor.is-focused-writing .manuscript-page.ms-scroll-container` only had `padding-bottom: 58px` — nowhere near enough blank space below the last line to pull the caret up into that band when typing at the very end of a long document, so it got pinned at the scroll maximum instead; increased to `50vh`. Separately, confirmed via instrumentation (temporary stack-trace/scroll-event logging, removed before commit) that the browser's own native caret-reveal scrolling on keyboard navigation (End/Home/arrows) runs its own ~150ms animation *after* the hook's correction and isn't governed by the container's `scroll-behavior` CSS, so it was winning and re-pinning the caret at the very bottom every time; added a debounced listener on the container's own `scroll` events that re-runs the comfort-zone check once native scrolling settles. Verified: full 68-test Playwright suite green (4 skipped, same as before), vitest suite (199 tests) and production build both clean. | Engineering/QA |

Fixed: `deleteNovel` now captures the filtered novels list and calls `saveAppData` immediately alongside `deleteProjectData`. | Closed — QA-verified 2026-08-28 | No further action needed for the local/session persistence layer this pass covered; a real-account pass against live Supabase (verifying the equivalent cloud-side immediate `deleteItem`/`deleteItemsByNovel` calls in `firestoreSync.js`, which this credential-free pass could not reach) is still worth a follow-up before final launch. | Note: this codebase has since moved to a per-table sync architecture (`nf_novels`/`nf_characters`/etc. each with their own debounced cloud push via `debouncedSaveItems`, plus an IndexedDB-backed local vault) rather than the single `user_data`/`saveAppData` blob this row's root-cause note describes — the specific function names above no longer exist, but the equivalent guarantee is in place in the current code: `deleteNovel` (`useStore.js`) commits the filtered novels list locally (synchronous, not debounced) and fires immediate (non-debounced) `deleteItem('novels', ...)` / `deleteItemsByNovel(...)` cloud calls rather than waiting on the per-table debounce; separately, `AuthContext`'s real (non-offline) `signOut` awaits `runSyncFlush()` — flushing any still-debounced cloud writes — before the session actually ends, as a second layer of protection. **2026-08-28 QA verification** (live browser, `VITE_OFFLINE_MODE=true`, since no live Supabase credentials are available in this environment — the cloud-delete calls above are stubbed out entirely in offline mode, so this pass exercised the local/session persistence layer, not the live cloud path): (1) created a project, deleted it, reloaded the page with zero delay (no wait for any debounce) — project stayed gone; (2) created a second project, deleted it, signed out (real `AuthContext.signOut` confirmation screen, "You've been signed out"), logged back in through the actual login form (offline mode accepts any credentials) — dashboard came back with 0 projects, the deleted project did not reappear. `npm run lint` (0 errors) and the full `vitest run` suite (409 tests) pass. |

Same-day provider compatibility fix: OpenAI/OpenRouter OpenAI-family models that reject `max_tokens` now receive `max_completion_tokens` instead. | Implemented, needs live-provider/browser QA | Browser-QA the AI panel with real configured providers: start a new chat, switch all four modes, verify fallback copy when no chapter/character is active, confirm estimates/source lists/costs update after changing provider/model/settings and project selection, send prompts against small and large projects, confirm usage details populate for providers that return token usage, verify Anthropic cache-control fallback with a supported and unsupported model if possible, verify an OpenAI/OpenRouter GPT-5 or o-series model can send without an unsupported `max_tokens` error, confirm no provider context-window errors on long chats, and spot-check that any error boundary page follows the active theme. | Engineering/QA |
| AI context selector omitted History | Users could not include World History entries as selected context for AI chat, so history-aware prompts had to rely on manual custom instructions. Fixed: AI chat context selection now includes a History section, selected history entries are counted in chat metadata, and selected entries are added to the system prompt with era/date/content details. | Fixed and QA-verified 2026-08-28, after fixing a live regression found during this pass — see fix note | No further action needed for the History-tab creation path this pass covered. Follow-up worth a look: editing a *pre-existing* chronicle entry that predates this fix (created before `createHistory: true`) won't retroactively gain a linked `worldHistory` record — `updateEvent` only syncs an already-linked entry, it doesn't create one. Not blocking (new entries are correct going forward; low volume today since this was effectively dead on arrival), but worth a small backfill/migration pass before launch if legacy chronicle entries need to show up as AI context too. | The AI context selector's "History" section (`AIPanel.jsx`'s `ContextSelector`, gated on `worldHistory.length > 0`) was implemented correctly and genuinely was reachable in isolation, but **the only user-facing "History" UI never populated `worldHistory` at all**, so the section had nothing to show and never rendered — a regression this QA pass found live (Playwright against a real offline-mode browser session, not code-reading) and fixed in the same session. Root cause: `src/components/worldhistory/WorldHistory.jsx` (the "History" tab) actually reads/writes **Timeline** data — it saves new entries via `addEvent(data, { createHistory: false })`, explicitly suppressing `addEvent`'s own default behavior of also creating a mirrored `worldHistory` record. `src/components/timeline/Timeline.jsx` (the plain "Timeline" tab) does the same, by design — ordinary plot beats shouldn't auto-populate world-history AI context. But since the *History* tab used the identical `createHistory: false` call, entries created there never linked a `worldHistory` record either, so `worldHistory` stayed permanently empty from normal use and the AI context selector's History section could never appear. Fix: `WorldHistory.jsx`'s new-entry save path now passes `{ createHistory: true }`, letting `addEvent`'s existing mirroring logic create the linked `worldHistory` record as originally intended; `Timeline.jsx` is untouched. Verified live: created a project, added a World History entry via Lore > History > New, opened AI Chat > New chat — before the fix, no History section appeared and the entry was uncounted despite displaying correctly in the History tab itself; after the fix, a "History (1)" section appears, era-grouped, the entry is selectable, the context count updates ("1 of 2 selected"), and "Start Chat · 1 item" reflects the selection. `npm run lint` (0 errors, 97 pre-existing warnings) and the full `vitest run` suite (409 tests) both pass unchanged. |

| Account switch can overwrite loaded project library with previous account's browser copy | Cross-account project data can appear under the wrong user and overwrite the populated account's project list; launch blocker for auth/data safety. | Fixed; needs real-auth QA | 2026-08-02: confirmed live on production — a stale/dormant project reappeared in a real account after create → logout → login on a browser that had previously been signed into a different account. RLS on `novels` is correctly scoped to `auth.uid()` (not a cross-tenant leak), and the owner guard (`nf_localOwner` match) was already correct. Root cause: `importData`'s "prefer local over cloud" freshness check (`src/store/useStore.js`) compared raw timestamps only — `remoteSavedAt` is derived from `MAX(updated_at)` of currently-existing cloud rows, which is not monotonic (deleting content, or an account sitting mostly-empty, can make it drop), so a long-dormant browser tab's old `nf_localWriteAt` could still beat it and get resurrected/re-pushed to Supabase. Fixed by capping the local-trust window to 30 minutes, since that check exists to protect a just-made edit from a slow cloud round-trip, not to revive old dormant data. All 308 unit tests pass (`npx vitest run`). `tests/e2e/account-isolation.spec.js` still covers: (1) foreign-owner data not loaded, (2) own projects persist on correct-owner reload, (3) localStorage tamper cleared safely by guard. Real two-account switch (A→B) with live Supabase accounts still needs manual QA to confirm the fix under real auth. | Engineering/QA |

| Import into an existing project and project merging | Product capability gap. AI Import and ZIP/manual import still primarily create separate projects; users need a destination/review/conflict-resolution flow to import into an existing project or merge projects safely. | Both halves now implemented — "import into an existing project" (`feature/import-into-existing-project`, merged to `main` via PR #23) works for YOW export ZIP, compatible structured ZIP, and AI-parsed import; **"merge existing projects" (source-project-to-source-project, not import-into) implemented 2026-09-02** as a one-way, non-destructive "pull another owned project's content into this one" action — see Owner/Notes for what it does and does not cover | Added a destination picker (`DestinationPicker` in `AIImportModal.jsx`) to the existing Import ZIP / AI Import flow: "a new project" (unchanged default) or any existing project. Deliberately scoped to pure append — new IDs via the existing `populateYowProject`/`populateProject` remap logic, no edits/deletes/overwrites of the destination's existing records, so there is no silent-overwrite risk this row originally flagged. Duplicate detection and skip/merge/replace choices are explicitly **not** built — every import lands as new records, so importing the same source twice will create duplicates rather than merge them; that layer, plus a dedicated project-to-project "merge" flow (as opposed to importing into an existing one), is still open scope. Two real risks were found in code review and fixed before this could be called safe: (1) `populateYowProject` (the native-YOW-export path only — `populateProject`, used by AI/archive imports, was already destination-type-aware) doesn't adapt to the destination's project type, so a comic-type export into a non-comic destination (or vice versa) would have written content into tables that project's workspace never renders — fixed by restricting the destination list to same-comic-ness projects only (`filterYowCompatibleDestinations`), with a stale-selection re-validation in `handleCreate` itself (not just the picker's filtered options) so a failed-import retry with a differently-typed file can't slip past it; (2) the destination list didn't respect a Free-plan account's single-editable-project lock (`membership.freeProjectId`), which every other write path in the app enforces — fixed (`filterImportableNovels`). Rollback-on-failure safety: only ever deletes a project this import itself just created (`isNewProjectImport`); an existing destination project chosen by the user is never deleted, even if left partially populated by a failed attempt. New unit tests for all four extracted helpers (`isNewProjectImport`, `filterYowCompatibleDestinations`, `filterImportableNovels`, plus existing coverage) and a new live-Playwright e2e spec (`tests/e2e/import-into-existing-project.spec.js`, added to `.github/workflows/qa.yml`'s smoke matrix) that creates two real projects, exports one, imports it into the other, and confirms both projects' content coexists with no third project created — run live against a real offline-mode dev server this session (not just code-reasoning), passing, alongside the full 419-test unit suite, 0 lint errors, and a clean production build. Two code-review passes found and fixed the type-mismatch and free-plan-lock gaps above; a third confirmation pass found nothing further. Not yet QA'd: the real-auth/live-provider AI Import path specifically (this session has no Supabase/AI-provider credentials — see the media-migration blocker row's same limitation), and a real Free-plan-account manual click-through (the free-lock filter is unit-tested but not exercised through the live picker, since offline dev mode doesn't simulate a locked account). **2026-09-02 (product-owner autonomous session, branch `feature/merge-existing-projects`): "merge existing projects" implemented.** New `MergeProjectModal.jsx`, opened from a "Merge another project in" card in the existing Project Settings dialog's new Import section (`Layout.jsx`'s `ProjectSettings`, reached via the in-workspace Studio settings gear). Deliberately reuses the already-reviewed `populateYowProject` + `filterYowCompatibleDestinations` machinery from the import-into-existing feature above, just sourced from `store.getProjectExportData(sourceId)` (an already-owned project's live data) instead of a parsed file upload — same safety envelope: purely additive, new IDs via the same remap logic, never edits/replaces/deletes anything in the destination, and the source project is only ever read, never written to. Comic/non-comic type compatibility is enforced the same way (`filterYowCompatibleDestinations`, applied in the reverse direction — filtering source candidates to match the fixed destination's comic-ness). No Free-plan-lock filtering was needed on the source list because Project Settings (both surfaces) only ever opens for a project the user can already edit — the destination is always the write-allowed side; reading an owned-but-locked project as a source is safe since merge never writes to the source. Explicitly still out of scope, same as the import-into-existing case: duplicate detection and a true bidirectional "combine two projects into one" flow — running a merge twice creates duplicate records rather than merging them, and there is no conflict-resolution UI. New unit/RTL tests (`MergeProjectModal.test.jsx`) cover candidate filtering (self-exclusion, comic-compatibility both directions) and the content-checklist/Merge-in-button enable state; a new live-Playwright e2e spec (`tests/e2e/merge-project.spec.js`, added to `.github/workflows/qa.yml`'s smoke matrix) creates two real projects, merges one into the other via the actual UI, and confirms the destination gained the source's content, the source project is completely untouched, and no third project was created — run live against a real offline-mode dev server this session (not just code-reasoning), passing, alongside the full 476-test unit suite, 0 lint errors, and a clean production build. Code-reviewed: found and fixed an unmemoized `getProjectExportData` call re-scanning the whole account's data on every checkbox toggle (now `useMemo`'d), and this roadmap row itself being stale (PR #23 already merged, not "pending" as this row previously said). No security-relevant surface changed (no new auth/RLS/webhook code; single-account, client-side-only data movement). | See the matching deferred-QA row in [docs/QA_PLAN.md](../docs/QA_PLAN.md) Priority 3 for what's still outstanding — that row also needs a 2026-09-02 update for the merge-specific coverage now in place. |

Fixed: `onAuthStateChange` now only clears `recoveryMode` on `SIGNED_OUT`; all other events update `user` without touching recovery state. Fixed 2026-06-26. | Fixed, needs QA | Click a real reset link from the new branded email, confirm the new-password screen appears rather than the app dashboard. Include in auth regression pass. | Engineering/QA |
| 2026-08-04: Manuscript tab crashed with "Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'length')" on a real user project | Full Manuscript-section crash (`SectionErrorBoundary`), 100% reproducible for the affected project, including on fresh import from a backup zip and in a private/incognito session (ruled out local browser cache and account/data corruption as the cause). Root-caused via the deployed bundle's minified stack trace plus a Safari retest, whose clearer error (`undefined is not an object (evaluating 'o[2].length')`) pointed at an array/capture-group index read rather than a bare property access — matching a unique `m[2].length` pattern in `renderInlineMarkdown`. That function guessed which markdown style (bold/italic/underline) a regex match was by checking whether the matched text *started with* `**`, instead of checking which capture group was actually populated. Unbalanced/mismatched asterisks in scene text (e.g. `**bold*`, a plausible real typo) make the *italic* alternative match while the raw text still starts with `**`, so the old code took the bold branch and read `.length` off the (undefined) bold capture group. Same bug duplicated in `FinalizedReader.jsx`'s copy of the function. | Fixed | Both copies of `renderInlineMarkdown` now branch on which capture group is defined (`m[2] !== undefined`, etc.) instead of sniffing the match text. Regression test (`SceneEditor.test.jsx`) added and confirmed to fail with the exact same error on the pre-fix code and pass after; full suite (331 tests) green. Fixed in commit `0120436`. Plain-language write-up in [BUG_FIXES.md](BUG_FIXES.md). | Engineering |
| 2026-08-09: AI chat panel felt laggy while the AI was replying | User-reported ("the ai chat is a bit laggy"). Root cause: `ChatView.send()` in `AIPanel.jsx` called `onUpdate()` on every single streamed chunk from the AI provider (tens of times per second — confirmed via `mockStreamMessage`'s 30ms/word cadence). Each call rewrote the entire chat-sessions array, which a parent `useEffect` then `JSON.stringify`-diffed against the persisted copy (`sameJson`) and, on any difference, pushed into `store.updateNovel()` → `commitLocal()` (`useStore.js`), which reads/diffs/writes the *entire* `nf_novels` collection — the same full-collection-write-per-keystroke pattern previously fixed for scene content (see the 2026-08 typing-lag rows above), just not yet applied to AI chat streaming. | Fixed | Streamed chunks now only update local component state (`liveMessage`, a cheap ChatView-only re-render used to overlay live text onto the message list for display); the session/global store is flushed at most once per 400ms plus always on stream completion or Stop, so a crash mid-stream loses at most one interval's worth of text. Verified in the offline dev build (`VITE_OFFLINE_MODE=true`, mock streaming): live text renders smoothly chunk-by-chunk, final content is complete and correct, and the session (title + full message) persists correctly across a page reload. `npx vitest run` and `npx eslint` both clean (no new issues). Needs a live-provider (non-mock) browser QA pass to confirm the perceived lag is gone with a real, faster-streaming API. | Engineering/QA |
| 2026-08-10: AI chat message actions felt clunky, no way to resend a failed reply, and streaming text scrolled jumpily | User-reported (screenshot of the AI chat panel): the per-message Copy button sat on its own row directly above the Idea/Lore row, reading as two separate, cramped button clusters; there was no way to resend a request after it failed (network error, or the "AI not configured" notice); and reply text scrolling into view felt jumpy while streaming. Root cause of the jumpiness: `ChatView`'s auto-scroll `useEffect` called `bottomRef.current?.scrollIntoView({ behavior: 'smooth' })` on every state change driven by `displayMessages`, which itself changed on every streamed chunk (`liveMessage.content`) — restarting a smooth-scroll animation tens of times per second. | Fixed, needs live-provider QA | `Message` (`src/components/ai/AIPanel.jsx`) now renders Copy, the new conditional Retry button, Idea, and Lore as one wrapping button row (`.ai-message-actions`, renamed from `.ai-response-save-actions` in `src/index.css`) instead of two stacked rows. Retry appears only on a failed assistant reply (`msg.error`) and calls a new `retry(failedMsgId)` in `ChatView`, which replaces just that message in place with a fresh streaming reply reusing the conversation up to that point (falls back to re-showing the AI-not-configured notice if no key is set); `send()` and `retry()` now share a `runAssistantStream()` helper. Auto-scroll was split into two effects gated on a `isNearBottomRef` scroll-position check (so scrolling up to reread history isn't yanked back down): one smooth-scrolls only when a message is appended (`messages.length`), the other does an instant `scrollIntoView({ behavior: 'auto' })` on streaming growth (`liveMessage?.content`) so the reply stays pinned to the bottom without restarting a scroll animation on every chunk. Verified in the offline dev build (`VITE_OFFLINE_MODE=true`, mock streaming): action row renders as a single non-overlapping group, and streaming text stays pinned to the bottom smoothly while typing out. `npx eslint` and `npm run build` both clean. Needs QA: trigger a real failed request (bad API key or network error) and confirm Retry re-sends and replaces the failed bubble; confirm Copy/Idea/Lore still work per message. | Engineering/QA |
| 2026-08-29: OpenRouter free-model/provider errors collapsed into a vague rate-limit message | User-reported: OpenRouter free models kept showing "The AI provider is rate-limiting requests — wait a moment and try again. (Provider returned error)", making it unclear whether the problem was the user's key/quota, OpenRouter's free-model cap, or an upstream provider capacity/rate limit. Root cause: `friendlyErrorMessage()` only received HTTP status + raw message, discarding OpenRouter's typed `error.metadata` and `Retry-After` header; `streamOpenAI()` also ignored OpenRouter mid-stream SSE error events. | Fixed; needs live-provider QA | Retest with a real OpenRouter free model that returns a 429/provider error and confirm the UI now explains upstream provider capacity vs platform rate limit, includes Retry-After timing when present, and suggests trying another `:free` model, waiting for reset, adding OpenRouter credits, or switching to a paid model variant. Also retest a mid-stream OpenRouter error if one can be induced. | `aiApi.js` now parses provider error bodies into status/message/metadata, preserves `Retry-After`, adds 402 credit-exhaustion copy, adds OpenRouter-specific free-model guidance, distinguishes upstream provider limits/capacity via `metadata.provider_code`/provider-returned wording, and surfaces OpenRouter mid-stream SSE errors instead of silently completing. Focused coverage added in `aiApi.test.js`; `npx vitest run src/utils/aiApi.test.js` and `npx eslint src/utils/aiApi.js src/utils/aiApi.test.js` pass. |
| 2026-08-29: Failed compact AI chat bar sends erased the submitted prompt | User-reported: when the bottom/compact AI chat bar request failed, the prompt field stayed empty, forcing the user to retype the message they had just sent. Root cause: `AIAssistant.send()` optimistically cleared `input` before streaming, but the `onError` and empty-response failure paths only set error state and never restored the captured submitted text. | Fixed; needs live-provider QA | Trigger a real compact-bar provider failure (bad key, network failure, or provider outage) and confirm the input contains the exact submitted prompt after the error; also confirm successful compact-bar answers still clear the input. | `AIAssistant.jsx` now clears stale error text at send start and restores the captured submitted prompt on provider error or no-response failure. Regression coverage added in `src/components/ai/AIAssistant.test.jsx`; `npm test -- src/components/ai/AIAssistant.test.jsx` passes. `npm run lint` exits 0 with only the existing warning backlog. |

## Change Control

When editing this roadmap:

- Keep Launch Required aligned to the final paid product promise.
- Move only explicitly excluded or future-business work to Future/Excluded.
- Move unvalidated ideas to Icebox.
- Add bugs only after reproduction or a concrete QA finding.
- Update linked generated artifacts only after this roadmap changes, not the other way around.
